Transgressions and Chern characters in coarse homotopy theory
Ulrich Bunke

Abstract
This paper investigates a variety of coarse homology theories and natural transformations between them. We in particular study the commutativity of a square relating analytical and topological transgressions with algebraic and homotopy theoretic Chern characters. Here a transgression is a natural transformation from a coarse homology theory to a functor which factorizes over the Higson corona functor, and a Chern character is a transformation from a K K -theory like coarse or Borel-Moore type homology theory to an ordinary version.



1
Introduction

1.1
Overview

The objects of coarse homotopy theory are
G
-bornological coarse spaces which will be analysed using equivariant coarse homology theories. The Higson corona functor
\partial_{h}
sends
G
-bornological coarse spaces to compact Hausdorff
G
-spaces whose homotopy theory will by studied using equivariant Borel-Moore homology theories.
Let
E^{G}
be an equivariant coarse homology theory and
F^{G}
be an equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory with the same target category.
A natural transformation

T^{G}:E^{G}\to F^{G}\circ\partial_{h}

(1.1)

of functors defined on
G
-bornological coarse spaces
will be called a transgression.
Currently we know two examples of transgressions. The first, the analytic transgression denoted by
T^{G,\mathrm{an}}
, relates the equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology
with the equivariant analytic
K
-homology. Its construction depends on the internal analytic details of the construction
of the
K
-theory functors.
The second, the topological transgression denoted by
T^{G,\mathrm{top}}
, connects the coarsification of
a Borel-Moore homology theory with the Borel-Moore homology itself. It is induced by a homotopy-theoretic construction using the strong excision property of Borel-Moore homology theory.

These two transgressions appear in the horizontal parts of the following diagram

111.46385pt\raise-250.4241pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 198.36934pt\raise-250.4241pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}})^{hG}(\partial_{h}X)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(1.2)

of functors and natural transformations evaluated at a
G
-bornological coarse space
X
. The functors on the left part and the middle are equivariant coarse homology theories. The functors on the right are Borel-Moore homology theories composed with the Higson corona functor.
Its vertical compositions involve Chern-character maps from versions of coarse or analytic
K
-homology to the coarsification of a Borel-Moore homology or the Borel-Moore homology itself, respectively.
Thereby the coarse algebraic Chern character is defined on
an algebraic coarse
K
-homology only, which is connected with the topological
K
-homology theory
by a comparison map
c^{G}
.
In our examples the data determining a
G
-equivariant coarse or Borel-Moore homology actually determines a family
of corresponding equivariant homology theories for all subgroups of
G
. The non-equivariant homology theory
associated to the trivial group in turn determines a Borel-equivariant version indicated by a superscript
hG
. In all our examples we have Borelification maps, i.e.,
natural transformations denoted by
\beta
from the original
G
-equivariant homology theory to the associated
Borel-equivariant version.

The existence of this diagram
allows to discuss the compatibility of the Chern characters with the
transgressions. There is no obvious reason that this diagram commutes.
It is one goal of
the present paper to
provide commutativity under certain additional assumptions and in the sense of
the extended diagram (
1.10
) below.
The diagram (
1.2
) is our interpretation of the diagram
[
EL25
, (1.2)]

in the framework of equivariant coarse homotopy theory as developed in
[
BE20b
]
,
[
BEKW20d
]
.

In
Section

1.2
we start with providing a rough description of the functors and transformations in (
1.2
). The complete details or references will be given in the subsections of
Section

2
.
It is one of the
goals of this paper is to produce a complete reference for constructions of the functors and transformations
in their natural generality.

Having explained the functors and transformations, in
Section

1.3
we
then extend the diagram to the diagram (
1.10
) and discuss the steps towards commutativity.
Proving commutativity means showing the existence of fillers.
We do not see technical relations between the algebraic and homotopy theoretic Chern characters

\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}
and
\mathrm{ch}^{hG}
, and also no relations between the analytic and topological transgressions

T^{G,\mathrm{an}}
and
(T^{\mathrm{top}})^{hG}
which could provide the desired fillers in general.

We approach the problem as follows. We first consider the case of the trivial group
G
and evaluate the diagram at
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(*)
, the cone over a point, see
3.1
. In this case we can calculate the compositions and normalize
\mathrm{ch}^{h}
such that the resulting diagram commutes. We then take advantage of the fact that we have a diagram of natural transformations between spectrum-valued functors which have homological properties. Using this we get a natural extension of the commutativity to cones over finite CW-complexes. By naturality this implies commutativity for the Borel-equivariant version on finite
G
-CW-complexes for finite groups.
Using the comparison of equivariant coarse homology theories with their Borel equivariant versions we conclude the commutativity of the diagram in the form (
1.14
) for finite groups and cones over finite
G
-CW-complexes.
In order to extend the commutativity further
we then introduce
an additional geometric construction leading to the motivic
transgression and extend the diagram further to (
1.16
).

The motivic transgression is an attempt to associate to every equivariant coarse homology theory
E^{G}
an associated Borel-Moore homology theory
F^{G}
and a transgression transformation (
1.1
). As a first approximation we propose to take
F^{G}:=E^{G}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}
and the motivic transgression
T^{\mathrm{mot}}
from (
3.9
). But note that
F^{G}
is not really a Borel-Moore homology, and
T^{\mathrm{mot}}
is not a natural transformation of functors defined on
G\mathbf{BC}
. One could ask for a better construction.

Note that our argument for commutativity is completely different from the arguments in

[
EL25
, (1.2)]
which are based on explicit calculations of the two
compositions in their diagram for special cycles. In contrast, our proof tries to max out the homotopical properties of the functors and transformations.

In
Section

3.9
we discuss the pairing of the right corners of the diagram (
1.2
) with
cohomology classes. We further derive the consequences of commutativity for the values of the pairings and state an analoge (
3.33
) of
[
EL25
, (1.1)]
.

Acknowledgement: The author was supported by the SFB 1085 (Higher Invariants) funded by the Deutsche Forschungsgemeinschaft (DFG). He thanks M. Ludewig and A. Engel for motivating discussions on early stages of
[
EL25
]
. He further thanks Th. Nikolaus for clarifying remarks on Borel-Moore (co)homology theories.
Finally he thanks B. Dünzinger for his patient interest and clarifying remarks on various pieces of this paper.

1.2
The functors and transformations

We start with the description of the functors and transformations in (
1.2
).

By
G\mathbf{BC}
we denote the symmetric monoidal category of
G
-bornological coarse spaces
[
BEKW20d
]
(
Section

2.1
).
The symbol
X
in (
1.2
) denotes a
G
-bornological coarse space.

The functors in the left and middle column in (
1.2
) are instances of equivariant coarse homology theories in the sense introduced in
[
BE20b
]
,
[
BEKW20d
]
.
These are functors

E:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-categories satisfying certain axioms
named in
2.4
. If
Y
is a
G
-bornological coarse space, then we can consider the twist

E_{Y}:=E(-\otimes Y):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

of
E
. Twisting preserves coarse homology theories. In
(
1.2
) we twist by the object
G_{can,max}
given the group
G
with the canonical coarse structure and the maximal bornology.

To any
C^{*}
-category with strict
G
-action
{\mathbf{C}}
which is effectively additive and admits countable
AV
-sums and to any object
A
in the stable
\infty
-category
\mathbf{E}
representing
E
-theory of
C^{*}
-algebras we can associate the equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology theory (
2.29
)

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}:={\mathbf{E}}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes A):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

This is a minor generalization of the construction of
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}

from
[
BE23
]
by adding the variable
A
in
\mathbf{E}
.
As the formula indicates it is constructed by applying
the topological
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories with coefficients in
A
to the functor which sends a
G
-bornological coarse space
X
to the Roe category
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)
from (
2.26
) of locally finite equivariant
X
-controlled objectsin
{\mathbf{C}}

[
BE23
]
. The equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology theory
is a
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
-valued and equivariant generalization of the functor which associates to a proper metric space the
K
-theory groups of the associated Roe algebra first introduced in

[
Roe93
]
.
The functor
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{G_{can,max}}
in (
1.2
) is obtained from
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}
by twisting with
G_{can,max}
and specializing to
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
and
A=\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})
.

We let

G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
denote the category of locally compact Hausdorff spaces with
G
-action and partially defined
G
-equivariant proper maps (
Section

2.3
). It contains the subcategory of compact Hausdorff spaces
G\mathbf{CH}
with
G
-action and everywhere defined maps. Associating to each bornological coarse space its Higson corona gives rise to
the Higson corona functor (
2.1
)

\partial_{h}:G\mathbf{BC}\to G\mathbf{CH}\ .

The compact Hausdorff
G
-space
\partial_{h}X
in (
1.2
) is thus the Higson corona of the
G
-bornological coarse space
X
.

Associated to the data
{\mathbf{C}}
and
A
as above we introduce
the equivariant analytic

K
-homology theory (
2.37
)

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}:=\mathbf{E}^{G}(C_{0}(-),{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\otimes A):G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

with coefficients in
({\mathbf{C}},A)
. As the formula shows,
it is constructed as the equivariant
E
-theory cohomology (with coefficients derived from
{\mathbf{C}}
and
A
) of the
C^{*}
-algebra of continuous functions on the space that vanish at infinity.
Here we use the
\infty
-categorical version of equivariant
E
-theory which was constructed in
[
BD24
]
. The latter is a homotopical version of the group-valued equivariant
E
-theory functor of
[
GHT00
]
.
The construction of the equivariant analytic
K
-homology

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}
with coefficients in
({\mathbf{C}},A)
is a minor generalization of
the constructions from
[
BELb
]
,
[
BL24
]
,
[
BELa
]
.
In contrast to the
KK
-theory version from
[
BELb
]
, a nice consequence of the present
E
-theory based construction is
that
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}
is an equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory (
2.98
).
Here we use that in contrast to
KK
the
E
-theory functor is unconditionally exact and preserves all filtered colimits.
The functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}
in (
1.2
) is obtained by specializing
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}
to
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
and
A=\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})
.

Using an analytic construction on the level of Roe categories inspired by
[
QR10
]
we define the analytic transgression transformation (
2.43
)

T^{G,\mathrm{an}}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A,G_{can,max}}\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\circ\partial_{h}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

The idea for its construction was already envisaged in the construction of the coarse corona pairing in
[
BL24
]
. Its technical details are closely related with the construction of the Paschke morphism in
[
BELa
]
.

At the moment we do not know a Chern character transformation from coarse topological
K
-homology
to a coarse periodic cyclic homology. In fact, the existence of such a transformation would have strong
implications explained in
2.70
. But we will construct a
coarse algebraic Chern character transformation whose domain is the equivariant
coarse algebraic
K
-theory functor with coefficients in
{\mathbf{C}}
(
2.49
)

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

This functor is obtained by composing the functor

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H({\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes^{\mathrm{alg}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}-):\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(1.3)

with the uncompleted
Roe category functor
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
from (
2.26
), where
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H
is a version of Weibel’s homotopy
K
-theory
[
Wei89
]
for
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}
is the algebra of trace class operators on a separable Hilbert space.
The functor
K{\mathcal{X}}_{G_{can,max}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
in (
1.2
) is the specialization of
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
to
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
, twisted by
G_{can,max}
. If
X
is presented by a proper metric space
X
, then the tensor product
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
is our (equivariant) version of the Roe algebra, often denoted by
{\mathcal{B}}_{X}
, of controlled and locally trace class operators considered, e.g., in

[
Roe93
]
,
[
Yu95
]
,
[
EL25
]
.

The equivariant
coarse algebraic
K
-theory functor
comes with a
natural comparison transformation (
2.50
)

c^{G}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(1.4)

to the topological equivariant coarse
K
-homology.
Here we omit the index
A={\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}
at
the target
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
.
The exactness and continuity properties of the homotopy
K
-theory functor
KH
are important
for verifying the coarse homology theory axioms for
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
.
On the other hand, the tensor product with the trace class operators in (
1.3
) is chosen as a compromise. On the one hand hand we want an interesting trace map to periodic cyclic homology. On the other hand
we want that the equivariant
coarse algebraic
K
-theory is an approximation of the topological equivariant coarse

K
-homology in the sense that the comparison map (
1.4
) is an equivalence on sufficiently finite
G
-bornological coarse spaces. For our choices this follows from
results of
[
CT08
]
, see
2.53
for a precise statement.

The target of the coarse algebraic Chern character is the coarse periodic cyclic homology theory (
2.64
)

\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})\ .

In order to construct this functor we start with composing the algebraic periodic cyclic homology functor
\mathrm{PCH}:\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})

with the uncompleted
Roe category functor
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
from (
2.26
).
The algebraic periodic cyclic homology functor is exact, but does not preserve filtered colimits. For this reason
we must restore
u
-continuity in a second step. Note that this second step was not needed in the cases of equivariant coarse cyclic or Hochschild homology constructed in a similar manner in
[
Cap20
]
.
The functor
\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}_{G_{can,max}}^{G}
in (
1.2
) is the specialization of
\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
to

{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
, twisted by
G_{can,max}
.

The coarse algebraic Chern character transformation (
2.69
)

\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}

is induced by the algebraic Chern character

\mathrm{ch}^{GJ}:K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H\to\mathrm{PCH}:\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})

(1.5)

and a trace map employing the trace of
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}
. The superscript
GJ
indicates that the origin of
\mathrm{ch}^{GJ}

is the Goodwillie-Jones trace
from the algebraic
K
-theory of rings
to the negative cyclic homology or rings. The Goodwillie-Jones trace induces
\mathrm{ch}^{GJ}

by forcing homotopy invariance
and the extension of these functors and transformations from rings to
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear additive categories.
The coarse algebraic Chern character transformation is a variant of the construction
of a Chern character from coarse algebraic
K
-theory with coefficients in additive categories to coarse cyclic or Hochschild homology given in
[
Cap20
]
.

For a commutative ring
k
the
equivariant ordinary coarse homology

\mathrm{H}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

with coefficient in a
k
-module
A
(see (
2.108
)) has been introduced in
[
BEKW20d
, Def. 7.2]
.
It is the obvious equivariant and dual version of the coarse cohomology invented by Roe
[
Roe93
]
.
It is represented by an explicit chain complex valued functor. Versions of this chain complex in special cases were also considered in
[
Yu95
]
,
[
Wul22
]
.

By periodizing we mean the application of the operation
\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}
.
Applying this to
\mathrm{H}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A)
and restoring
u
-continuity we obtain
the periodic equivariant coarse homology
functor with coefficients in
A
(
2.76
)

\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)\ .

The functor
\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}_{G_{can,max}}^{G}(-,{\mathbb{C}})
in (
1.2
)
is the specialization of
\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A)
to
k=A={\mathbb{C}}
, twisted by
G_{can,max}
.

We now assume in addition that the
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
has a
G
-invariant trace. It induces
a natural trace transformation (
2.118
)

\tau:\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,{\mathbb{C}}):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})\ .

(1.6)

The map

\beta:\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}_{G_{can,max}}^{G}(-,{\mathbb{C}})\to\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}_{G_{can,max}}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}(-,{\mathbb{C}})

is the Borelification map
from
(
2.111
) and the map

{\mathtt{pr}}_{X}:\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}_{G_{can,max}}(-,{\mathbb{C}})\to\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}(-,{\mathbb{C}})

is induced by the projection
(-)\otimes G_{can,max}\to(-)
.

If
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a functor to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
, then

E\mathbf{P}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

denotes the result of its coarsification introduced in
2.132
.
The construction of coarsification has been introduced by Roe
[
Roe03
, Sec. 5.5 ]
, and in the background we use its homotopical version
[
BE20a
]
.
If
E
is a weak equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory, then its coarsification
E\mathbf{P}

is by
2.133
an equivariant coarse homology theory.

In the non-equivariant case, for any ring spectrum
R
we introduce a functor
2.104

which send a
R
-module spectrum
M
to a Borel-More homology theory

M_{\mathrm{BM}}:\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)

with
M_{\mathrm{BM}}(*)\simeq M
.
The formation of Borel-equivariant homology theories allows to construct equivariant Borel-Moore homology theories from non-equivariant ones. In particular
we can form the Borel-equivariant Borel-Moore homology

(M_{\mathrm{BM}})^{hG}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)\ ,

see
2.112
.
The functor
(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}})^{hG}\mathbf{P}
in the lower left corner of (
1.2
)
is an instance of the composition of the last three constructions for
R=H{\mathbb{C}}
.

Staying in the non-equivariant case,
for any
k
-module
A
we have the
Borel-Moore character (
2.142
)

\chi_{\mathrm{BM}}:\mathrm{H}{\mathcal{X}}(-,A)\to HA_{\mathrm{BM}}\mathbf{P}:\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

relating the ordinary coarse homology with coefficients in
A
and the coarsification of the Borel-More homology

HA_{\mathrm{BM}}
. Specializing to
k=A={\mathbb{C}}
,
its periodization (see (
2.161
)) and Borelification is the transformation (
2.162
)

P(\chi_{\mathrm{BM}})^{hG}:\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}(-,{\mathbb{C}})\to(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}}\mathbf{P})^{hG}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})

appearing in (
1.2
).

The trace transformation (
1.6
) and the composition

P(\chi_{\mathrm{BM}})\circ\tau:\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}})\mathbf{P}:\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})

from coarse periodic cyclic homology to a version of ordinary coarse homology theory
are the general homotopy theoretic analogues of characters considered, e.g., in
[
Yu95
, Sec. 2]
,
[
Cap19
, Sec. 4.3]
,
[
LT25
, (4.1.3)]
,
[
EL25
, (2.2)]
.

Using the strong excision axiom, for
any weak equivariant Borel-Moore theory
E^{G}
, in
2.144
we describe the topological transgression, a natural transformation

T^{G,\mathrm{top}}:E^{G}\mathbf{P}\to\Sigma E^{G}\circ\partial_{h}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

With different technical details the fact that strong excision allows to define such a transgression
has first been observed in
[
Wul22
]
.
In the diagram (
1.2
) we use the Borel-equivariant version
(T^{\mathrm{top}})^{hG}
from (
2.168
)
of the topological transgression.

In the non-equivariant case and for
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
in

2.108
we show (the at least for the author surprising fact) that the analytic
K
-homology with coefficients in
A
has
a completely homotopy theoretic description:

Proposition 1.1
.

We have an equivalence of spectrum-valued functors

K^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}\simeq K(A)_{\mathrm{BM}}:\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(1.7)

The proof of this result essentially uses the recent classification of Borel-Moore cohomology theories by D. Clausen
(see
[
KNP
, Thm. 3.6.13]
,
Theorem

2.99
).
The
1.1
allows to construct
the homotopical Chern character
\mathrm{ch}^{h}

in (
1.2
) as the natural transformation (
2.111
)

\mathrm{ch}^{h}:K^{\mathrm{an}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gerfwereg354eewrgw}}}{{\simeq}}K({\mathbb{C}})_{\mathrm{BM}}\to\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}}:\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(1.8)

obtained by applying
(-)_{\mathrm{BM}}
to
the map of spectra

K({\mathbb{C}})\simeq KU\to KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\simeq}}\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}\ .

(1.9)

It depends on the choice of the identification of
H{\mathbb{C}}
-modules marked by
!
.

Remark 1.2
.

The homotopical Chern character (
1.8
) as a natural transformation from (non-equivariant) analytic
K
-homology to periodic complex Borel-Moore homology theory defined on all of
\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
is new, and its unconditional existence was surprinsing to the author.
Classical constructions, e.g. in
[
Con85
]
,
[
CM90
]
,
[
CM95
]
, associated a Chern character with values in variants of cyclic homology to special cycles
in analytic
K
-homology of
X
which can be represented by suitably summable Fredholm modules.
The existence of good cycles usually require some sort of smoothness of
X
.
For smooth manifolds the cyclic homology can further be mapped to de Rham
homology and finally to periodic complex Borel-Moore homology. Our homotopical Chern character therefore allows to formulate index theorems
stating that the homotopical Chern character of some class is equal to an analytically defined Chern character of the same class in cases where the latter is defined. In the present paper will not pursue this direction.
But this information would be important if one wanted to deduce the results of
[
EL25
]
from the present paper in a technical sense.

∎

If
E:BG\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathbf{BC},{\mathcal{C}})
is a non-equivariant coarse homology theory
with
G
-action in the sense of
2.13
, then according to
2.14
we can form
the Borel-equivariant coarse homology theory

E^{hG}:=(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}E)^{u}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ ,

where for
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
the group
G
acts on
E(X)
diagonally via its action on
E
and the action on
X
by functoriality, and
(-)^{u}
indicates the operation of forcing
u
-continuity. A natural transformation
\phi:E\to F
of functors as above gives rise to a transformation
\phi^{hG}:E^{hG}\to F^{hG}
. In all our examples the data determining the equivariant coarse homology theory
E^{G}
also determines
a non-equivariant coarse homology theory
with
G
-action
E
, and these two are related by a canonical Borelification map
\beta:E^{G}\to E^{hG}
.
The details will be given in
Section

2.2
.

This finishes the rough description of the functors and transformations featuring (
1.2
).

1.3
Commutativity

In this section we explain how we derive step by step commutative diagrams from (
1.2
).
The main results are
1.4
,
1.5
and
1.6
stated below.

We first simplify the right part by projecting from the Higson corona to a simpler space.
We consider the functor

\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}:G\mathbf{UBC}\to G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}

(see (
2.23
)) defined, using Gelfand duality, such that

C_{0}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y))\simeq C_{u,0}(Y)
, where the right-hand side is the
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra of bounded uniformly continuous functions on
Y
which become arbitrary small outside of sufficiently large bounded subsets.
The Higson corona of a
G
-bornological space, if not empty, is a complicated compact Hausdorff
G
-space. In order to simplify the lower right corner in (
1.2
) we consider

Y
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
and a map
\pi:\partial_{h}X\to\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y)
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
.
Then we can extend the diagram (
1.2
) as follows:

\hskip-28.45274pt\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 1.99306pt\hbox{\kern 43.81227pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-25.33923pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.99306pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}_{G_{can,max}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.88957pt\raise 5.08492pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.08492pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{c^{G}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 97.81227pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise (1.10)

where the two right squares commute by naturality of
\beta
and
\mathrm{ch}^{hG}
. It is even closer to
the diagram
[
EL25
, (1.2)]
.

We let
\phi
be the clockwise composition and
\psi
be the counterclockwise composition

\phi,\psi:K{\mathcal{X}}_{G_{can,max}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\Sigma(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}})^{hG}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y))\ .

(1.11)

Note that
\psi
and
\phi
depend on
X
and the map
\partial_{h}X\to\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y)
, but this fact is not reflected in the notation.
Our goal is to find
an equivalence

\phi\simeq\psi\ ,

i.e., a filler of the big cell.

We use the cone-at-
\infty
functor described in
3.1
.
We have a natural transformation

\pi:\partial_{h}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\to\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}:G\mathbf{UBC}\to G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}

(1.12)

which will be described in detail after (
3.18
) in
Section

3.5
.
So inserting
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Z)
in place of
X
and
Z
in place of
Y
the whole diagram (
1.10
) becomes the diagram (
1.13
) of natural transformations between functors defined on
G\mathbf{UBC}
.

We now proceed with the following steps.

1.

We consider the case of the trivial group. Then the diagram simplifies to (
1.13
)

(a)

We evaluate at
Z=*
.
In this case we can calculate the compositions and normalize the identification (
1.9
) such that (
1.13
) commutes. The detailed argument is given in
3.3
.

(b)

We then take advantage of the facts that
K{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))
and
(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}}\mathbf{P})(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(-))
are homotopy invariant and excisive functors on
\mathbf{UBC}
(see
3.2
for the first), and that (
1.13
) is a diagram of natural transformations of spectrum valued functors. We conclude by

3.34
that
(
1.13
) naturally commutes for
Z
a finite CW-complex, see
1.4
.

2.

We can now generalize to the equivariant case.

(a)

If
G
is finite and if
Z
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
is homotopy invariant to a finite
G
-CW-complex, then
we will conclude the commutativity of the Borel-equivariant version (
1.14
) of (
1.13
). More precisely, the Borel-equivariant coarse homology theories in this diagram are naturally the versions
E^{\{e\},hG}
, in contrast to
E^{hG}
derived from
E
associated to
E^{G}
as in
2.17
. But in view of
2.22
we can replace
E^{\{e\},hG}
by
E^{hG}
.
By homotopy invariance of the functors in (
1.13
) we can assume that
Z
is a
G
-finite
CW
-complex. Then it is a finite CW-complex with a cellular
G
-action. Using the natural commutativity
obtained in
Item

1b
the diagram (
1.13
) becomes a commutative diagram in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{Sp})
. We then apply
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}
and restore
u
-continuity (see
2.11
)
where necessary in order to get the commutative diagram (
1.14
).

(b)

We now use the comparison of the equivariant and Borel equivariant coarse homology theories in order to conclude that the outer part diagram of the diagram (
1.15
) commutes provided
G
is finite and
Z
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
is homotopy equivalent to a finite
G
-CW-complex.
In order to see this we use that all squares involving
\beta
commute. The big inner cell is precisely (
1.14
)
which commutes as seen in
2a
.
The squares ①, ②, ③ can be refined to compositions of two squares, one involving

{\mathtt{pr}}
and the other involving
\beta
. The first type of square commutes by naturality of the respective transformation.
For the commutativity of the cells involving the Borelification
\beta
we refer to

•

2.55
and (
2.78
) for ①,

•

2.84
and
2.83
for ②,

•

and
2.73
and
2.74
for ③.

In each case we must combine a square of the kind (
2.21
)
with a square of the type (
2.22
).
The commutativity of ④ is shown in

2.46
.

(c)

We finally introduce an additional geometric data leading to the motivic transgression.
In this case we get a diagram (
1.16
). We assume that
G
is finite and that
Y
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
is homotopy invariant to a finite
G
-CW-complex.
The maps

p^{G,\mathrm{an}}
and
p^{G,\mathrm{top}}
are the analytical and topological Paschke maps (
3.13
) and (
3.23
).
The Paschke maps are versions of
T^{G,\mathrm{an}}
or
T^{G,\mathrm{top}}
for cones, respectively. So it is not surprising that
by
Theorem

3.12
and
Theorem

3.18

the squares and triangles involving Paschke maps commute.
The big inner cell in (
1.16
) is precisely the outer cell of (
1.15
) which is already seen to commute by

Item

2b
. We conclude
1.5
.

Proposition 1.3

(
3.3
)
.

There is a unique choice of the marked equivalence in (
1.9
) such that the diagram (
1.13
) commutes for
Z=*
.

By
3.34
we get

Corollary 1.4
.

If
Z
in
\mathbf{UBC}
is a finite
CW
-complex, then
the diagram (
1.13
) commutes naturally in
Z
.

Corollary 1.5
.

If
G
is finite,
X
is in
G\mathbf{BC}
, and
Y
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
is homotopy invariant to a finite
G
-CW-complex, and

\pi:\partial_{h}X\to\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y)
is such such that the motivic transgression
T^{\mathrm{mot}}
exists, then (
1.10
) commutes.

The following is a consequence of
3.24
and
1.5
.
Note that if
Y
is a compact Hausdorff space, then it is an object of
\mathbf{UBC}
in the canonical way and

\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y)\cong Y
.

Corollary 1.6
.

Assume that
G
is the trivial group.
If
X
is a metrizable bornological coarse space and
\bar{X}
is a metrizable Higson-dominated compactification of
X
by a finite CW-complex
Y
, then (
1.10
) commutes.

See also
3.22
and
3.23
for more general conditions implying the existence of the motivic transgression.

Example 1.7
.

We consider
X={\mathbb{R}}^{n}
with the standard bornological coarse structure induced by the metric.
It has a metrizable Higson-dominated compactification by the sphere
S^{n-1}
. Since
S^{n-1}
has the structure of a finite CW-complex, by
1.6

the diagram (
1.10
) commutes for
X={\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
\partial_{h}{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to S^{n-1}=:Y
.

∎

Remark 1.8
.

The square (
1.16
) commutes as a diagram of functors on the category of triples
(X,Y,\partial_{h}X\to\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y))
, where
Y
is a finite CW-complex.
∎

Remark 1.9
.

In
[
EL25
, Thm. B]

it is assumed that
X
is presented by a complete Riemannian manifold
and
\bar{X}

is a metrizable
Higson-dominated compactification with boundary
Y
such that
(\bar{X},\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y))
is a finite
CW
-pair. In this case

1.6
applies. So by our result we drop the smoothness assumption on
X
. Furthermore we only need that
Y
is a finite CW-complex and have no finiteness assumption on
\bar{X}
.

∎

2
Functors and natural transformations

2.1
Bornological coarse spaces and the Higson corona functor

A bornological coarse space is a set equipped with a coarse structure and a compatible bornology. A morphism between bornological coarse spaces is a map of sets which is controlled (images of coarse entourages are coarse entourages) and proper (preimages of bounded subsets are bounded). We let
\mathbf{BC}
denote
the
symmetric monoidal category of bornological coarse spaces. For details we refer to
[
BE20b
, Sec. 2]
.

We let
\mathbf{CH}
denote the category of compact Hausdorff spaces and continuous maps. By Gelfand duality it is equivalent to the opposite of the category
{\mathbf{C}^{\ast}\mathbf{Alg}}_{\mathrm{comm}}
of commutative unital
C^{*}
-algebras and unital homomorphisms.

In the following we explain the Higson corona construction which provides a functor

\partial_{h}:\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{CH}\ .

We recall the notation from
[
BL24
, Def. 3.1]
.
For a set
X
we consider the
C^{*}
-algebra
\ell^{\infty}(X)
of bounded functions

f:X\to{\mathbb{C}}
with the supremum norm.
For
f
in
\ell^{\infty}(X)
, an entourage
U
of
X
, and a subset
Y
we define the
U
-variation of
f
on
Y
by

\mathrm{Var}_{U}(f,Y):=\sup_{(x,x^{\prime})\in U\cap Y\times Y}|f(x)-f(x^{\prime})|\ .

If
X
is a bornological coarse space with coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}
and bornology
{\mathcal{B}}
, then
we consider the unital
C^{*}
-algebra

\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X):=\{f\in\ell^{\infty}(X)\mid\forall U\in{\mathcal{C}}\colon\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{B\in{\mathcal{B}}}\mathrm{Var}_{U}(f,X\setminus B)=0\}

(2.1)

of bounded functions whose
U
-variation becomes arbitrary small
outside of sufficiently large bounded subsets for every coarse entourage
U
.
The
C^{*}
-algebra

\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}}):=\{f\in\ell^{\infty}(X)\mid\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{B\in{\mathcal{B}}}\sup_{x\in X\setminus B}|f(x)|=0\}

(2.2)

of bounded functions which themselves become arbitrary small
outside of sufficiently large bounded subsets is an ideal in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X)
.
We then form the commutative unital
C^{*}
-algebra

C(\partial_{h}X):=\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X)/\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\ .

(2.3)

Definition 2.1
.

Higson corona
\partial_{h}X
in
\mathbf{CH}
is defined to be the compact Hausdorff space corresponding to

C(\partial_{h}X)
by Gelfand duality .

Note that a morphism
X\to X^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{BC}
functorially induces via pull-back a morphism of exact sequence
of the associated commutative
C^{*}
-algebras

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 167.77249pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 234.37576pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern We get a functorially induced map between the Higson coronas

\partial_{h}f:\partial_{h}X\to\partial_{h}X^{\prime}\ .

This finishes the construction of the Higson corona functor.

Lemma 2.2
.

The Higson corona functor is coarsely invariant in the sense that if
f
is a coarse equivalence, then

\partial_{h}f
is a homeomorphism.

Proof.

If
f,g:X\to X^{\prime}
are close maps between bornological coarse spaces, then
\partial_{h}f=\partial_{h}g
.
∎

Let
G
be a group.
Recall from
[
BEKW20d
]
that a
G
-bornological coarse space is a bornological coarse space with an action of
G
by automorphisms such that the coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}
contains a cofinal subset
{\mathcal{C}}^{G}
of invariant entourages. The symmetric monoidal category
G\mathbf{BC}
of
G
-bornological coarse spaces
forms a full subcategory

\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{BC})

(2.4)

of the symmetric monoidal category of bornological coarse spaces with an action of
G
and equivariant morphisms.
By functoriality, the Higson corona functor induces a functor

\partial_{h}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{BC})\to G\mathbf{CH}\ ,

where
G\mathbf{CH}
is
the category of compact Hausdorff spaces with
G
-action and equivariant continuous maps. The latter is equivalent to the opposite of

G{\mathbf{C}^{\ast}\mathbf{Alg}}_{\mathrm{comm}}
by Gelfand duality.

Example 2.3
.

The Higson corona of a bounded bornological coarse space is empty.
Furthermore, the Higson corona of a discrete bornological coarse space
with the minimal bornology is the boundary of the Stone-Čech compactification
of the set considered as a discrete topological space.
∎

2.2
Equivariant coarse homology theories

In this section we recall the notion of an equivariant coarse homology theory. We further introduce the associated non-equivariant coarse homology theory and the corresponding Borel-equivariant coarse homology theory. Using the additional structure of transfers we construct the Borelification map.

Let

E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

be some functor. Following
[
BEKW20d
]
we adopt the following definition.

Definition 2.4
.

The functor
E^{G}
is an equivariant coarse homology theory if
{\mathcal{C}}
is a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category and
E^{G}
is coarsely invariant, excisive,
u
-continuous and vanishes on flasques.

Remark 2.5
.

An equivariant coarse homology is called strong if it in addition annihilates weakly flasques.
We refer to
[
BEKW20d
]
for a detailed explanation of these conditions.
The property of strongness is relevant when we consider the composition
E^{G}\circ{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}:G\mathbf{UBC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

since it ensures that this composition is not only homotopy invariant, excisive and
u
-continuous, but also vanishes on flasques and is therefore a local homology theory in the sense of
[
BE20a
]
.
∎

Note that the conditions for an equivariant coarse homology theory only involve colimits.

Corollary 2.6
.

If
\phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
is a colimit-preserving functor between cocomplete stable
\infty
-categories and
E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is an equivariant coarse homology theory, then the composition
\phi\circ E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
is again an equivariant coarse homology theory.

If
E^{G}
is strong, then so is
\phi\circ E^{G}
.

Example 2.7
.

Recall from
[
BEKW20d
, Sec. 4]
that there is a universal equivariant coarse homology theory

\mathrm{Yo}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to G\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}\ .

(2.5)

By design, for every cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
pull-back along
\mathrm{Yo}^{G}
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{Yo}^{G,*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}(G\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{\mathcal{X}}}(G\mathbf{BC},{\mathcal{C}})\ ,

where the superscript
{\mathcal{X}}
indicates the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{BC},{\mathcal{C}})
of equivariant coarse homology theories.
If
E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is an equivariant coarse homology theory, then we will use the same symbol for the corresponding colimit-preserving functor
E^{G}:G\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.
∎

Remark 2.8
.

Assume that
{\mathcal{C}}
is a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category.
The full subcategory
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{\mathcal{X}}}(G\mathbf{BC},{\mathcal{C}})
of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{BC},{\mathcal{C}})
of equivariant coarse homology theories is closed under colimits. If
{\mathcal{C}}
is presentable, then
{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{BC},{\mathcal{C}})
is also presentable and we
have an adjunction

\mathrm{incl}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{\mathcal{X}}}(G\mathbf{BC},{\mathcal{C}})\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{BC},{\mathcal{C}}):R\ .

The functor
R
thus provides a universal way approximate any functor
E:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
by an equivariant coarse homology theory

RE:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
. The counit of the adjunction is a natural transformation
RE\to E
with the universal property that for any coarse homology
F:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
with a natural transformation
F\to E
we have a unique factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.90973pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 30.90973pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{RE\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.72395pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.90973pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.39778pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.7448pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 34.7448pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(2.6)

In general it is complicated to control
RE
. But in the case that
E
is already coarsely invariant, excisive, and vanishes on flasques, then
R
just forces
u
-continuity, and in this case we have an explicit formula, see
2.11
.

∎

Example 2.9
.

We consider the Higson corona functor
\partial_{h}:G\mathbf{BC}\to G\mathbf{CH}
from
2.1
. Let
E:G\mathbf{CH}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be any functor to a presentable stable
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
. Then by
2.8
we can form an equivariant coarse homology theory

R(E\partial_{h}):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

It comes with a natural transformation

R(E\partial_{h})\to E\partial_{h}
. In this generality it is difficult to understand the functor
R(E\partial_{h})
.

The analytic transgression considered in
Section

2.6
has a factorization (
2.63
)
which can be used to show that
R(K_{{\mathbf{C}},A,G_{can,max}}^{G,\mathrm{an}}\partial_{h})
is non-trivial by showing that

T^{G,\mathrm{an}}
is non-trivial.

Similarly we can use the factorization (
2.169
) of the topological transgression introduced in
Section

2.16
in order to show that

R(E\partial_{h})
is non-trivial for a weak equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.

It seems to be an interesting problem in coarse homotopy theory to understand the coarse homology theories of the form
R(E\partial_{h})
in greater detail.

∎

Example 2.10
.

If
Y
is a
G
-bornological coarse space and
E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is an equivariant coarse homology theory, then using the symmetric monoidal structure of
G\mathbf{BC}
we can define a new equivariant coarse homology theory

E^{G}_{Y}:=E^{G}(-\otimes Y):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ ,

called the twist of
E^{G}
by
Y
. We refer to
[
BEKW20d
]
for justifications.
∎

Construction 2.11
.

Sometimes we want to post-compose coarse homology theories with exact functors which may not preserve filtered colimits. Then coarse invariance, excision and vanishing on flasques or weakly flasques is preserved, but not
u
-continuity.
In this case we can apply
the construction
F\mapsto F^{u}
of forcing
u
-continuity
[
BE20b
, Sec. 6.1]
in order to
obtain again a coarse homology theory. If
{\mathcal{C}}
is presentable, then this construction is special case of the application of the functor
R
from
2.8
.

We form the category
G\mathbf{BC}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
of pairs
(X,U)
of
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
and
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}
.
A map

f:(X,U)\to(X^{\prime},U^{\prime})
is a morphism
f:X\to X^{\prime}
in
G\mathbf{BC}
such that
f(U)\subseteq U^{\prime}
.
We consider
a functor
F:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
to a cocomplete
\infty
-category.
We can then consider the diagram of functors

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 18.27843pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.27843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-24.77557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.60114pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(X,U)\mapsto X_{U}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.27843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.27843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.27843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 126.62694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 126.62694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 60.20723pt\raise-21.62585pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{F^{u}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 126.62694pt\raise-1.59467pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 42.27843pt\raise-32.08115pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 84.45268pt\raise-32.08115pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 130.23805pt\raise-32.08115pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

where
X_{U}
is the
G
-bornological coarse space defined by equipping the underlying

G
-bornological space of
X
with the coarse structure generated by
U
, and the functor

F^{u}
is defined by left Kan-extension.
We say that
F^{u}
is obtained by forcing
u
-continuity.
By the point-wise formula we have

F^{u}(X)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}F(X_{U})\ .

(2.7)

The functor
F^{u}
inherits the following properties of
F
: coarse invariance, excision, vanishing on flasques.
In addition,
F^{u}
is
u
-continuous by design.
The natural transformation

G\mathbf{BC}^{{\mathcal{C}}}\ni(X,U)\mapsto(X_{U}\to X)\in{\mathbf{Fun}}(\Delta^{1},G\mathbf{BC})

induces a natural transformation

F^{u}\to F:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

(2.8)

which has the following universal property: If
E
is
u
-continuous, then the transformation

\mathrm{Nat}(E,F^{u})\to\mathrm{Nat}(E,F)

induced by (
2.8
)
is an equivalence.

∎

We will apply this construction as follows.

Assume that
{\mathcal{C}}
is a stable, complete and cocomplete
\infty
-category, and that

E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is an equivariant coarse homology theory.

Definition 2.12
.

The
2
-periodization of
E^{G}

is defined by

P(E^{G}):=(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}E^{G})^{u}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

The functor
\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}E^{G}

is coarsely invariant, excisive and vanishes on flasques, but it is no longer
u
-continuous in general since the infinite product does not commute with filtered colimits. For this reason we force
u
-continuity in the second step.

For every
k
in
{\mathbb{Z}}
we have a projection

P(E^{G})\simeq(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}E^{G})^{u}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gwezghuowerfwerf}}}{{\to}}\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}E^{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{pr}}_{k}}}{{\to}}\Sigma^{2k}E^{G}\ .

(2.9)

Let
G
be a group and

E:BG\times\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

(2.10)

be a functor to a complete
\infty
-category.
Then we can form

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}E:G\mathbf{BC}\xrightarrow{\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G},\eqref{vweujiofwvwefewfvf}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{BC})\xrightarrow{E}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG\times BG,{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{diag}}_{BG}^{*}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}}{\mathcal{C}}\ .

Definition 2.13
.

If the adjoint
BG\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathbf{BC},{\mathcal{C}})
of
E
in (
2.10
) takes values in coarse homology theories,
then we say that
E
is a coarse homology theory with
G
-action.

In this case
the functor
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}E
is coarsely invariant, excisive and vanishes on flasques, but it is no longer
u
-continuous in general since the limit over
BG
does not commute with filtered colimits unless
BG
is finite. In order to retain an equivariant coarse homology theory we force
u
-continuity in the second step.

Definition 2.14
.

We define the Borel-equivariant coarse homology theory associated to a coarse homology theory
E
with
G
-action by

E^{hG}:=(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}E)^{u}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

If

\phi:E\to F:BG\times G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

is a natural transformation between coarse homology theories with
G
-actions, then
we get a functorially induced natural transformation

\phi^{hG}:E^{hG}\to F^{hG}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

(2.11)

between their associated Borel-equivariant versions.

Recall that a morphism between
G
-bornological coarse spaces is a weak coarse equivalence if it becomes a coarse equivalence after forgetting the
G
-action. The following is an immediate consequence of the definition.

Corollary 2.15
.

The Borel-equivariant coarse homology theory
E^{hG}
associated to
E

sends weak coarse equivalences to equivalences.

Example 2.16
.

The projection map

X\otimes G_{max,max}\to X

(2.12)

is a weak coarse equivalence, but not a coarse equivalence unless the
G
-action on the underlying set of
X
is free.

Let

G\mathbf{BC}\to G\mathbf{BC}_{h}

be the quotient which identifies close maps. By
[
Hei21
]
it represents the Dwyer-Kan localization of
G\mathbf{BC}
at the coarse equivalences.
The object
G_{max,max}
with the projection
G_{max,max}\to*
and the diagonal

G_{max,max}\to G_{max,max}\otimes G_{max,max}
is an idempotent commutative coalgebra in
G\mathbf{BC}_{h}
.
This coalgebra induces a right Bousfield localization of
G\mathbf{BC}_{h}
which inverts the weak coarse equivalences.
The map (
2.12
) is the component of the counit of this localization at
X
.

In Diagram (
1.2
) we twist some equivariant coarse homology theories with
G_{can,max}
. If
G
is finite, then
G_{can,max}\cong G_{max,max}
. In this case twisting by
G_{can,max}
is the universal way to
make the equivariant coarse homology theory weakly coarsely invariant.

∎

Let us now start from an
equivariant coarse homology theory

E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

Then, using
2.10
, we define a coarse homology theory with
G
-action by

E:BG\times\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ ,\quad X\mapsto E(X):=E^{G}(\mathrm{Res}_{G}(X)\otimes G_{min,min})\ ,

where the
G
-action comes from the right-action of
G
on
G_{min,min}
and
\mathrm{Res}_{G}

equips
X
with the trivial
G
-action.

Definition 2.17
.

We call
E
the non-equivariant coarse homology theory associated to
E^{G}
.

To
E
we can further associate the Borel-equivariant coarse homology theory
E^{hG}
according to
2.14
.
In order to relate
E^{G}
with
E^{hG}

we now assume that
E^{G}
extends to an equivariant coarse homology theory with transfers
E^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}
in the sense of
[
BEKW20c
]
:

\textstyle{G\mathbf{BC}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}}

(2.13)

Here
G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}}
is the
(2,1)
-category of spans

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 72.36806pt\raise-25.76468pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.36806pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

where
f:W\to X
is a morpism in
G\mathbf{BC}
which is in addition bornological, and
g
is a controlled and bornological map which is in addition a bounded coarse covering, see
[
BEKW20c
]
for details. We use a dashed arrow in order to make clear that
g
is in general not a morphism in
G\mathbf{BC}
since it may not be proper.

Example 2.18
.

If
S
is a
G
-set, then the projection

\textstyle{X\otimes S_{min,min}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{pr}}_{X}}
\textstyle{X}

is a typical example of a bounded coarse covering.
In fact, locally on coarse components any bounded coarse covering over
X

is of this form.

In contrast, if
G
is non-trivial, then the morphism (
2.12
) is not a bounded coarse covering.

∎

Construction 2.19
.

Under the assumption that
E^{G}
has an extension
E^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}
as in (
2.13
) we
construct a natural transformation

\beta:E^{G}\to E^{hG}\ .

(2.14)

Recall that

E^{hG}\simeq(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\circ E\circ\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G})^{u}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

In view of the universal property of
(-)^{u}\to\mathrm{id}
(see
2.11
) and the fact that
E^{G}
is
u
-continuous we must construct a map

E^{G}\to E^{hG}:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\circ E\circ\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

We will actually construct its adjoint

\underline{E^{G}}\to E\circ\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}})\ .

Its value at
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
is a map

\underline{E^{G}(X)}\to E^{G}(\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(X)\otimes G_{min,min})\ .

(2.15)

In
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,G\mathbf{BC})
we have a canonical isomorphism

X\otimes G_{min,min}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(X)\otimes G_{min,min}\ ,\quad(x,g)\mapsto(g^{-1}x,g)\ ,

(2.16)

where
G
acts on
G_{min,min}
by right multiplication.
The transfer is a map

\mathrm{tr}:X\to X\otimes G_{min,min}

(2.17)

in
G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}}
represented by the span

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\otimes G_{min,min}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 123.19011pt\raise-31.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\otimes G_{min,min}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

We claim that it refines to an equivariant map

(\mathrm{tr},\rho):\underline{X}\to X\otimes G_{min,min}

(2.18)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}})
.
For every
g
in
G
we have a two-isomorphism
\rho_{g}:\mathrm{tr}\to g\circ\mathrm{tr}
given by

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.36241pt\raise-39.69441pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 154.0178pt\raise-93.83331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-125.11108pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 62.36241pt\raise-125.11108pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 123.19011pt\raise-125.11108pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\otimes G_{min,min}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

The family
\rho=(\rho_{g})_{g\in G}
satisfies a cocycle condition.
Applying the extension
E_{\mathrm{tr}}^{G}
of
E^{G}
to the map

\underline{X}\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{tr},\rho)}X\otimes G_{min,min}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gwerhfiuhwerifewrfwer}}}{{\simeq}}\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(X)\otimes G_{min,min}

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}})
we get the desired map (
2.15
) in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}})
.
This construction is natural in
X
.

This finishes the construction of the natural transformation (
2.14
).

∎

Definition 2.20
.

If
E^{G}
is an equivariant coarse homology theory with a given extension
E^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}
, then we call
the map

\beta:E^{G}\to E^{hG}

in (
2.14
)
the Borelification map.

Note that the Borelification map depends on the choice of the extension
E^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}
.

Let

\phi^{G}:E^{G}\to F^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

be a natural transformation between equivariant coarse homology theories.
Then we get an induced natural transformation

\phi:E\to F:BG\times G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

(2.19)

of associated coarse homology theories with
G
-action.
We then let

\phi^{hG}:E^{hG}\to F^{hG}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

(2.20)

denote the induced transformation of Borel-equivariant coarse homology theories. The following is an immediate consequence of the constructions.

Corollary 2.21
.

If
\phi^{G}
extends to a natural transformation

\phi^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}:E^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}\to F^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}:G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}}\to{\mathcal{C}}

between equivariant coarse homology theories with transfers, then the following square commutes:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 10.34215pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.34215pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.3495pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\beta_{E}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.34215pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.02516pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-10.27272pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.41158pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 34.41158pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F^{hG}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(2.21)

Note that the filler of this square may depend on the choice of
\phi^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}
.

Remark 2.22
.

In our examples we have for every group
G
a family
(E^{H})_{H\subseteq G}
of coarse homology theories
E^{H}:H\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
for all subgroups of
G
.
We than have two versions of a non-equivariant coarse homology theory, namely
E
as defined in
2.17
and the member
E^{\{e\}}
of the family. In our examples the coarse homology theories
E^{H}
furthermore depend on the choice of some parameters on which
G
acts. In particular, we get a
G
-action on
E^{\{e\}}
from the action on the parameters.

In all of our examples we will show that there is a canonical equivalence

E^{\{e\}}\simeq E:BG\times\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

The natural transformations in our examples also come in families

(\phi^{H}:E^{H}\to F^{H})_{H\subseteq G}

of natural transformations. The transformation
\phi^{G}
induces
\phi:E\to F
according to (
2.19
).
We will furthermore show that
the squares

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{E^{\{e\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\{e\}}}
\textstyle{F^{\{e\}}}

(2.22)

commute.
These facts allow to simplify the notation an not to distinguish between
E
and
E^{\{e\}}
anymore.

∎

2.3
Uniform bornological coarse spaces, topological bornological spaces, and locally compact Hausdorff spaces

A partially defined equivariant proper map
X\to Y
between locally compact Hausdorff
G
-spaces is a pair
(U\subseteq X,U\to Y)

of an invariant open subset
U
of
X
and an equivariant
proper continuous map
U\to Y
.
The category
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
of locally compact Hausdorff
G
-spaces and partially defined equivariant proper maps
is by Gelfand duality equivalent to the opposite of the category
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{comm}}
of possibly non-unital commutative
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras. It contains the wide subcategory
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}
of everywhere defined equivariant proper maps.
Under Gelfand duality the latter corresponds to the wide subcategory of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{comm}}
of essential homomorphisms.

A
G
-uniform bornological coarse space is a
G
-bornological coarse space with an additional
G
-uniform structure which is compatible with coarse structure. A morphism
between
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces is a morphism of
G
-bornological coarse spaces which is in addition uniformly continuous. We have a symmetric monoidal category

G\mathbf{UBC}
of
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces, see
[
BE20b
]
,
[
BE20a
]
,
[
BEKW20d
]
for details.

A
G
-bornological topological space is a
G
-topological space with an additional compatible
G
-invariant bornology. A morphism between
G
-bornological topological spaces is an equivariant continuous map which is in addition proper in the bornological sense.
We have the symmetric monoidal category

G\mathbf{BornTop}
of
topological bornological coarse spaces. We refer to
[
BE20b
]
for details.

These categories are related by functors

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 29.04918pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.17426pt\raise
