53.04918pt\raise-31.91168pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-29.04918pt\raise-63.82336pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G\mathbf{BornTop}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.95074pt\raise-41.82092pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.0466pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota^{\mathrm{top}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.41287pt\raise-37.41168pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces .

(2.23)

The functor

\iota
forgets the uniform structure, and
\iota^{\prime}
forgets the coarse structure and takes the underlying topological space presented by the uniform space.
We will often omit the symbols
\iota
and
\iota^{\prime}
.
The other functors
are characterized via Gelfand duality as follows:

1.

C_{0}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{\ell^{\infty}}(X))=\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}}_{X})

2.

C_{0}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(X))=C_{u}(X)\cap\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}}_{X})=:C_{0,u}(X)

3.

C_{0}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}(X))=C(X)\cap\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}}_{X})=:C_{0}(X)
.

Here
C(Z)
denotes the
C^{*}
-algebra of bounded continuous functions on the topological space
Z
with the supremum norm. If
Z
is a uniform space, then

C_{u}(Z)
denotes the subalgebra of
C(Z)
of uniformly continuous functions.
For
Z
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
the
C^{*}
-algebra

C_{0}(Z)
denotes the subalgebra of the
C^{*}
-algebra
C(Z_{+})
of functions on the one-point compatification
Z_{+}
which satisfy
f(+)=0
.

The triangles in (
2.23
) do not commute but are filled with natural transformations

\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{\ell^{\infty}}\circ\iota\to\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}\ ,\qquad\iota^{\mathrm{top}}\circ\iota^{\prime}\to\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}

induced by the obvious inclusions of
C^{*}
-algebras indicated by the morphisms in the left column in (
2.24
) below.

For
X
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
we have canonical morphisms of exact sequences

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{0,u}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{u}(X)\cap\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{C_{u}(X)\cap\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X)}{C_{0,u}(X)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{!!}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{0}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(X)\cap\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{C(X)\cap\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X)}{C_{0}(X)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{!}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(\partial_{h}X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(2.24)

It has been shown in
[
BL24
, Rem 3.5]
that the vertical map marked by
!
is an isomorphism
provided
X
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
has a paracompact underlying topological space.

Corollary 2.23
.

If
X
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
has a paracompact underlying topological space, then
we have a compactification
\overline{\iota^{\mathrm{top}}X}
of
\iota^{\mathrm{top}}X
by the coarse Higson corona

\partial_{h}X
.

Here by definition
\overline{\iota^{\mathrm{top}}X}
is the Gelfand dual of
C(X)\cap\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X)
.
The vertical map in (
2.24
) marked by
!!
is an isomorphism only under rather restrictive conditions, see
[
BL24
, Prop. 3.22]
.

We have a fully faithful functor

\beta:G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}\to G\mathbf{BornTop}

(2.25)

which equips a locally compact Hausdorff space with the bornology of relatively compact subsets.

2.4
Equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology

In this section we recall the construction of the equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology functor.
We further compare two versions of its Borelification.

Let
G
be a group. We let

C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denote the category of possibly non-unital
C^{*}
-categories and consider the category

GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}:={\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})

of possibly non-unital
C^{*}
-categories with strict
G
-action and equivariant functors. We refer to
[
Bun24
]
for details.

We fix
{\mathbf{C}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and assume that the underlying
C^{*}
-category is
effectively additive and admits countable AV-sums
[
BE
]
.
Following
[
BE23
]
we can then consider the functor

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}

which associates to
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
the
C^{*}
-category of equivariant locally finite
X
-controlled objects in
{\mathbf{C}}
.

Remark 2.24
.

At various places in the present paper we need some details of the
description of the functor
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
. Let
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
denote the multiplier category of
{\mathbf{C}}
(we refer to
[
BE
]
for details) and consider

X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
. The objects of
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
are triples

(C,\rho,\nu)
, where
C
is an object of
{\mathbf{C}}
,
\rho=(\rho_{g})_{g\in G}
is a cocycle of unitaries

\rho_{g}:C\to gC
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
, and
\nu
is an equivariant finitely additive multiplier projection-valued measure on
X
.
Thereby we require that for every
x
in
X
the projection
\nu(\{x\})
belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}
and has an image, that
\mathrm{id}_{C}=\sum_{x\in X}\nu(\{x\})
in the strict topology on
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
,
and that for every bounded subset
B
of
X
the set

\{x\in B\mid\nu(\{x\})\not=0\}
is finite.

A controlled morphism
A:(C,\rho,\nu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
is an equivariant morphism
A:C\to C^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}

with the property that there exists a coarse entourage
U
of
X
such that

\nu(\{x^{\prime}\})A\nu(\{x\})=0
for all
(x,x^{\prime})
in
(X\times X)\setminus U
.
The composition and the involution of controlled morphisms are inherited from
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
.

The objects described above and controlled morphisms form the unital
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear
*
-category

{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
which happens to be additive. The norm on the morphism spaces of
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
induces a
norm on the morphism spaces of
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
and we define the unital
C^{*}
-category

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
as the completion.

If
f:X\to X^{\prime}
is a morphism in
G\mathbf{BC}
, then we define functors

f_{*}:{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X^{\prime})
and

f_{*}:{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X^{\prime})

on objects and morphisms by

f_{*}(C,\rho,\nu):=(C,\rho,f_{*}\nu)\ ,\quad f_{*}A:=A\ .

For justifications and more details we refer to
[
BE23
]
.
We thus have functors

{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ ,\qquad{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\ .

(2.26)

where in the first case we forget the
*
-structure and consider the values of
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
as a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear additive categories.

∎

We next recall the
E
and
K
-theory functors for
C^{*}
-categories. By
[
Joa03
]
we have an adjunction

A^{f}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\leftrightarrows C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{incl}

where
\mathrm{incl}
views a
C^{*}
-algebra as a
C^{*}
-category with a single object.

By
[
BD24
]
we have a universal
homotopy-invariant,
K
-stable, exact, and filtered colimit-preserving functor

\mathrm{e}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{E}

(2.27)

to a stable
\infty
-category which turns out to be presentable.
If we equip
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
with the symmetric monoidal structure given by the maximal tensor, then

\mathbf{E}
has a unique presentably symmetric monoidal structure such that
\mathrm{e}
admits a unique symmetric monoidal refinement.
The functor in (
2.27
) is an
\infty
-categorical version of the classical
E
-theory functor
of
[
Hig90
]
,
[
CH90
]
.

We have an adjunction

b:{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\leftrightarrows\mathbf{E}:K

(2.28)

where
K:={\mathtt{map}}_{\mathbf{E}}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}},-)
is the
K
-theory functor, and
the essential image of the fully faithful functor
b
is called the bootstrap class.

Let

H:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathcal{C}}

be some functor.
Following
[
BE
]
we adopt the following definition.

Definition 2.25
.

The functor
H

is called a finitary homological functor
if
{\mathcal{C}}
is a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category and
H

sends unitary equivalences to equivalences,
exact sequences to fibre sequences, and preserves filtered colimits.

Lemma 2.26
.

The composition

\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{A^{f}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}}\mathbf{E}

(2.29)

is a finitary homological functor.

Proof.

In order to cite results from
[
BELb
]
we use the
factorization

\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{KK}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}}

where
\mathrm{kk}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{KK}
is the
\infty
-categorical version of Kasparov’s
KK
theory
[
Kas88
]

constructed in
[
BELb
]
and
c
is the canonical comparsion map induced by the universal property of
\mathrm{kk}
.
We therefore have an equivalence
\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}\simeq c\circ\mathrm{kk}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
,
where
\mathrm{kk}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}:=\mathrm{kk}\circ A^{f}
.
By
[
BELb
, Thm. 1.32.2]
the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
sends unitary equivalences to equivalences.
Consequently
\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
does so.

We have a functor
A:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{i}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
(see
[
Bun24
, Sec. 6]
), where

C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{i}
is the wide subcategory of
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of functors which are injective on objects. We further have a natural transformation

A^{f}_{|C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{i}}\to A:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{i}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ .

By

[
BELb
, Prop. 6.9]
the morphism
\mathrm{kk}(A^{f}({\mathbf{C}}))\to\mathrm{kk}(A({\mathbf{C}}))
is an equivalence for every
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
. This implies that
\mathrm{e}(A^{f}({\mathbf{C}}))\to\mathrm{e}(A({\mathbf{C}}))
is an equivalence, too.

Since
A
preserves exact sequences by
[
Bun24
, Prop. 8.9.2]
and
\mathrm{e}
is exact by design we can conclude that

\mathrm{e}\circ A
sends exact sequences to fibre sequences.
Hence so does
\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
.

Finally,
\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
preserves filtered colimits, since
A^{f}
, being a left-adjoint, preserves all colimits and

\mathrm{e}
preserves filtered colimits by design.
∎

Recall the
2.4
of an equivariant coarse homology theory.
The following is a consequence of
2.26
and
[
BE23
, Thm. 7.3]
.

Corollary 2.27
.

The composition

\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}}{{\to}}C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{E}

(2.30)

is an
\mathbf{E}
-valued equivariant coarse homology theory.

By
[
BE23
, Thm. 7.4]
it is in addition strong.

We write
\mathbf{E}(-,-):={\mathtt{map}}_{\mathbf{E}}(-,-)
and
omit the symbols
\mathrm{e}
or
\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
if we insert
C^{*}
-algebras or
C^{*}
-categories.

Let
A
be in
\mathbf{E}
.

Definition 2.28
.

The functor

K_{A}:=\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},-\otimes A):\mathbf{E}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

(2.31)

is called the
K
-theory with coefficients in
A
.

Since
\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})
is a compact object in
\mathbf{E}
the functor
K_{A}

preserves colimits.
The following definition is therefore justified by
2.6
.

Definition 2.29
.

We define
the equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology with coefficients in
({\mathbf{C}},A)
by

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}:G\mathbf{BC}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle K_{A}}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

Remark 2.30
.

If
A={\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}
, then we omit the subscript and write

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:=K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}}
for the equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology with coefficients in
{\mathbf{C}}
from

[
BE23
]
.
If
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
with the trivial action, then we further omit the subscript
{\mathbf{C}}
and call

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}:=K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

the equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology.
Finally, if
G
is the trivial group, then we omit the symbol
G
from the notation and call

K{\mathcal{X}}:=K{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\}}:\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

(2.32)

the topological coarse
K
-homology.

For a comparison of
K{\mathcal{X}}
(or
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
) with classical constructions as
K
-theory of (equivariant) Roe
algebras associated to proper metric spaces (with
G
-action) we refer to
[
BE20b
, Sec. 8.7]
,
[
BE25
, Sec. 7]
.
∎

Remark 2.31
.

The additional parameter
A
in
\mathbf{E}
can be used to introduce coefficients.
For example we could consider the object

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}:=b(KU\wedge H{\mathbb{Q}})

with
b
as in (
2.28
).
The map
KU\to KU\wedge H{\mathbb{Q}}
in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
induces a morphism
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}\to{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}
in
\mathbf{E}
and
a rationalization map

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathbb{Q}}}\ .

But note that
as long as
A
belongs to the bootstrap class (the essential image of
b
), or, more generally, to the Künneth class in
\mathbf{E}
, we have an equivalence

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\simeq K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes_{KU}K(A)

so that the additional parameter does not really give new information.
This is in contrast to the case of analytic
K
-homology considered in
Section

2.5
, see
2.38
.

At the moment we do not have an interesting application for the case where
A
does not belong to the Künneth class.
∎

We now develop the facts envisaged in
2.22
.
For the moment (in contrast to the convention adopted in (
2.32
)) let
\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
and
K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}
denote the non-equivariant coarse homology theories associated to
\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
and
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}
according
2.17
. Further we let
\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
and

K{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}
denote the specializations of (
2.30
) and
2.29
to the case of the trivial group. These functors carry
G
-actions induced by the
G
-action on
{\mathbf{C}}
.

In the following we show these these two versions of non-equivariant coarse homology theories are equivalent.

Let
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}
denote the
2
-category of unital
C^{*}
-categories, functors and unitary equivalences. The functor
\ell:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}
presents the Dwyer-Kan localization of
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
at the unitary equivalences
[
Bun19
]
.
If
F:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a functor which sends unitary equivalences to equivalences, then
we obtain a canonical factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 18.41382pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.41382pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(2.33)

Proposition 2.32
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all
AV
-sums, then we have canonical equivalences

\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\simeq\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\{e\}}:BG\times\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{E}

(2.34)

and

K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\simeq K{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}:BG\times\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

(2.35)

Proof.

Let
F
be one of
\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
or
K_{A}\circ\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
.
These functors send unitary equivalences to equivalences and therefore have
factorizations
F_{2,1}
as in (
2.33
), and the functors in questions can be written in the form

F\circ{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\simeq F_{2,1}\circ\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ ,\quad F\circ{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\{e\}}\simeq F_{2,1}\circ\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ .

Here
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\{e\}}
is the functor from (
2.26
) for the trivial group and the
G
-action induced from the action on
{\mathbf{C}}
, and in analogy to
2.17

{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:={\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X\otimes G_{min,min})

(2.36)

with the
G
-action induced by the right action of
G
on
G_{min,min}
.

The asserted equivalences come from an equivalence

\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:BG\times\mathbf{BC}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}

(2.37)

which we now describe.
For
X
in
\mathbf{BC}
we first define a functor

\phi_{X}:{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\ .

(2.38)

For every object
(C,\rho,\nu)
of
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)={\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X\otimes G_{min,min})
we choose an image
C_{e}\to C

of the projection
\nu(X\times\{e\})
. It exists by the assumption that
{\mathbf{C}}
is effectively additive.
We write
\nu_{e}
for the restriction of
\nu
to
C_{e}
and
X\times\{e\}
.
We then let
\phi_{X}
in (
2.38
) send
(C,\rho,\mu)
to
(C_{e},\nu_{e})
in
{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
.
Furthermore, for

A:(C,\rho,\nu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
we let

A_{e}:C_{e}\to C\xrightarrow{A}C^{\prime}\to C_{e}^{\prime}
be the restriction of
A
, where
the last map in this composition is the adjoint of the isometry
C_{e}^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}
.
This is the image of
A
under the functor
\phi_{X}
in (
2.38
).
This finishes the description of
\phi_{X}
from (
2.38
). In order to show that it is an equivalence
one can construct an inverse which sends an object
(C,\nu)
in
{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
to an object
(\bigoplus_{g\in G}gC,\rho,\tilde{\nu})
in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)={\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X\otimes G_{min,min})
, where

\rho=(\rho_{g})_{g\in G}
with
\rho_{g}:\bigoplus_{h\in G}hC\to g\bigoplus_{h\in G}hC

sends the summand with index
h
canonically to the summand with index
g^{-1}h
of
g\bigoplus_{h\in G}hC
.
The measure
\tilde{\nu}
is given by
\tilde{\nu}(\{(x,g)\}):=g\nu(\{x\})
on the summand with index
g
.
It furthermore sends
A:(C,\nu)\to(C^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
to
\bigoplus_{g\in G}gA:\bigoplus_{g\in G}gC\to\bigoplus_{g\in G}gC^{\prime}
.
Note that this construction requires the existence of AV-sums of cardinality of
G
.

Since the images of the projections
\nu(X\times\{e\})
involve a choice we can only extend the construction above to produce a natural transformation of functors

{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:\mathbf{BC}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}\ .

(2.39)

To this end, if
f:X\to X^{\prime}
is a morphism,
(C,\rho,\nu)
is an object of
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)

and
C_{e}\to C
and
C_{e}^{\prime}\to C
are the images chosen for

\nu(X\times\{e\}
and
(f_{*}\nu)(X\times\{e\})
, then
we have a canonical unitary isomorphism
\sigma_{f,(C,\rho,\nu)}:C_{e}\to C_{e}^{\prime}
.
The family of unitaries
\sigma_{f}:=(\sigma_{f,(C,\rho,\nu)})_{(C,\rho,\nu)\in{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)}
is a natural unitary isomorphism

\sigma_{f}:f_{*}\circ\phi_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\phi_{X^{\prime}}\circ f_{*}\ .

The pair
((\phi_{X})_{X},(\sigma_{f})_{f})

is a natural transformation of functors (
2.39
).

We finally identify the
G
-actions.
The composition
\phi_{X}\circ g
would send

(C,\rho,\nu)
to
(C_{g},\nu_{g})
, where

C_{g}
is the choice of an image of
\nu(X\times\{g\})
and
\nu_{g}
is the corresponding restriction.
The composition
g\circ\phi_{X}
sends

(C,\rho,\nu)
to
(gC_{e},g\nu_{e})
.
The maps

\kappa_{g,(C,\rho,\nu)}:C_{g}\to C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho_{g}}}{{\to}}gC\to gC_{e}

provide an isomorphism

\kappa_{g}:=(\kappa_{g,(C,\rho,\nu)})_{(C,\rho,\nu)\in{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}(X):\phi_{X}\circ g\to g\circ\phi_{X}\ .

The family
\kappa:=(\kappa_{g})_{g\in G}
satisfies a cycle condition and the pair

(\phi_{X},\kappa):{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)

is a refinement of (
2.38
) to a
G
-equivariant functor.
For a morphism
(g,f)
in
BG\times\mathbf{BC}
the following square

\textstyle{C_{g}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{f,(C,\rho,\nu))}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{g,(C,\rho,\nu)}}
\textstyle{C^{\prime}_{g}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{g,(C,\rho,f_{*}\nu)}}
\textstyle{gC_{e}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g\sigma_{f,(C,\rho,g_{*}\nu))}}
\textstyle{gC_{e}^{\prime}}

commutes which expresses the compatibility of
\sigma
and
\kappa
. Alltogether this describes the transformation (
2.37
).
∎

By
[
BE23
, Thm. 10.4]
, if
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all
AV
-sums, then the functor
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
from (
2.26
) has an extension

\textstyle{G\mathbf{BC}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}}
\textstyle{{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\textstyle{{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}}
\textstyle{G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}},\mathrm{tr}}^{G}}

(2.40)

to the
(2,1)
-category of
G
-bornological coarse spaces with transfers.
Postcomposing with the functors
(e^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}})_{2,1}

or

K_{A}\circ(e^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}})_{2,1}
we get extensions

This allows to construct
the Borelification maps

\beta:\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ ,\quad\beta:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}

(2.41)

according to

2.20
.

In the following we construct versions

\beta^{\prime}:\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\mathrm{e}{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ ,\quad\beta^{\prime}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}

(2.42)

of the Borelification maps and argue that they are equivalent to the maps in (
2.41
) via the equivalences from
2.32
. This is relevant since we will show later that the analytic transgression is compatible with the Borelification using the version
\beta^{\prime}
, see
2.46
.

The maps
\beta^{\prime}
are induced by a natural transformation

\beta^{\prime\prime}:\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}\ .

(2.43)

We then apply
F_{1,2}
and post-compose with the canonical map
F_{1,2}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}F_{1,2}
, where
F
is one of
\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
or
K_{A}\circ\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
, and lift against a map (
2.8
).

In order to construct (
2.43
) note
that objects of

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
are triples

(C,\rho,\nu)
where
\rho=(\rho_{g})_{g\in G}
is a cocyle of unitary isomorphism
\rho_{g}:(C,\nu)\to(gC,g_{*}\nu)
in

{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
, and the morphisms
A:(C,\rho,\nu)\to(C,\rho,\nu)
of

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
are
morphisms
A:(C,\nu)\to(C^{\prime},\nu)
in
{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
such that
gA\rho_{g}=\rho^{\prime}_{g}A
. This is almost the description of the
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)
given in
2.24
. The only difference is that the morphisms

\rho_{g}
for objects in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)
are controlled by
{\mathtt{diag}}(X)
, while
here they are just approximable by controlled ones.
We therefore have a natural fully faithful embedding inducing (
2.43
).
Note that the adjoint of
\beta^{{}^{\prime\prime}}
is described explicitly below in (
2.58
).

Remark 2.33
.

Note that in
[
Bun19
]
we introduced marked
C^{*}
-categories in order to write

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
as a limit of
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\{e\}}(X)
. To this end we mark the
{\mathtt{diag}}(X)
-controlled unitaries in the latter.

∎

Lemma 2.34
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all
AV
-sums. The following triangle of natural transformations of functors
G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1})
commutes:

\textstyle{\underline{\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{tr},\rho),\eqref{gweoiroifjoewfwerfwerferwferfrffw}}
\textstyle{\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
2.37
)
\textstyle{\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\{e\}}}

(2.44)

Proof.

The left-down map in the triangle is induced by applying
\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}
to the map (
2.18
). The right-down map is the adjoint of (
2.43
).
This is a direct
2
-categorical calculation unfolding all definitions.
∎

We apply the functors
\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
or
K_{A}\circ\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
, go over to adjoints,
and form the lifts under (
2.8
) using the
u
-continuity of the upper corners in order to get:

Corollary 2.35
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all
AV
-sums.
The following triangles commute:

(2.45)

2.5
Equivariant analytic
K
-homology

In this section we recall the construction of the equivariant analytic
K
-homology.
We furthermore introduce its Borel-equivariant version and the Borelification map.

Generalizing (
2.27
) to the equivariant case
we let

\mathrm{e}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{E}^{G}

(2.46)

be the universal homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable, exact and filtered colimit preserving functor to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category constructed in
[
BD24
]
, called the equivariant
E
-theory functor
Again,
\mathbf{E}^{G}
is presentable, and if we equip
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
with the symmetric monoidal structure given by the maximal tensor, then

\mathbf{E}^{G}
has a unique presentably symmetric monoidal structure such that
\mathrm{e}^{G}
admits a unique symmetric monoidal refinement.
The functor in (
2.46
) is an
\infty
-categorical refinement of the functor introduced in
[
GHT00
]
.

We consider the equivariant generalization

\mathrm{e}^{GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{A^{f}}G\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}^{G}}\mathbf{E}^{G}

(2.47)

of (
2.29
) which again sends unitary equivalences to equivalences, is exact and preserves filtered colimits by the equivariant versions of the arguments given in the proof of
2.26
.
As these properties are not needed in the present paper we will not discuss the details further.

We let
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denote the
G
-
C^{*}
-category constructed from
{\mathbf{C}}
in

[
BELa
, Def. 2.15.(2)]
.
Its objects are pairs
(C,\rho)
which extend to an object
(C,\rho,\nu)
in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)
for some

X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
with a free
G
-action. The morphisms

A:(C,\rho)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
are morphisms
A:C\to C^{\prime}

in
{\mathbf{C}}
, and the involution and composition is inherited from
{\mathbf{C}}
.
Finally, the
G
-action fixes the objects, and
g
in
G
acts on
A
by

A\mapsto g\cdot A:=\rho_{g}^{\prime,-1}\circ gA\circ\rho_{g}
.

Remark 2.36
.

In the classical approach to Kasparov’s
KK
-theory
[
Kas88
]
, for
a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
one considers the standard
A
-Hilbert module
L^{2}(G,A)
with
G
-action
and its
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
K(L^{2}(G,A))
of compact operators.
It fits into an exact sequence

0\to K(L^{2}(G,A))\to B(L^{2}(G,A))\to Q(L^{2}(G,A))\to 0\ ,

(2.48)

where
B(L^{2}(G,A))\cong MK(L^{2}(G,A))
is the
algebra of bounded adjointable
operators on the standard
A
-Hilbert module and also the multiplier algebra of
K(L^{2}(G,A))
, and
Q(L^{2}(G,A))
is an equivariant and Hilbert
A
-module version of the classical Calkin algebra.

The category
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
can be considered as the analogue the
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
K(L^{2}(G,A))

in the realm of
G
-
C^{*}
-categories. In analogy to (
2.48
) it fits into an exact sequence

0\to{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to 0

(2.49)

of
G
-
C^{*}
-categories, see
[
BELa
, Def. 2.15.]
, where
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is the multiplier category of
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.

∎

We write
\mathbf{E}^{G}(-,-):={\mathtt{map}}_{\mathbf{E}^{G}}(-,-)
and omit the symbols
\mathrm{e}^{G}
or
\mathrm{e}^{GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}

if we insert
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras or categories. Implicitly we we also use the functor
\mathrm{Res}_{G}:\mathbf{E}\to\mathbf{E}^{G}
in order to consider objects of
\mathbf{E}
as objects of
\mathbf{E}^{G}
.

In the following we consider the functor

C_{0}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{comm}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{op}}\ .

Recall that
A
belongs to
\mathbf{E}
.

Definition 2.37
.

The equivariant analytic
K
-homology with coefficients in

({\mathbf{C}},A)
is defined by

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}:=\mathbf{E}^{G}(C_{0}(-),{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\otimes A):G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

In
2.98
we will show that the equivariant analytic
K
-homology with coefficients in

({\mathbf{C}},A)
is an equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory.

Remark 2.38
.

Since the functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}
is symmetric monoidal we have a natural transformation

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes_{KU}K(A)\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\ ,

but this transformation is not an equivalence in general.
The functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}
sends disjoint unions (also infinite ones) to products.
In contrast, if
K(A)
is not dualizable in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
, then the functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes_{KU}K(A)

does not have this property. So in the case of analytic
K
-homology we have, e.g., two non-equivalent versions of a rationalization,

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\wedge H{\mathbb{Q}}\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathbb{Q}}}\ .

We prefer to work with the second one since it is again an equivariant Borel-Moore homology homology theory, while the first is not.
The same applies if we replace
{\mathbb{Q}}
by
{\mathbb{C}}
.

∎

Recall the notation from
2.31
.

Definition 2.39
.

The natural transformation of functors

\mathrm{ch}^{K}:K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathbb{C}}}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(2.50)

induced by

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}\to{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}

is called the
K
-theoretic Chern character.

Note that
G
acts on
K^{\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}:\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
via its action on
{\mathbf{C}}
.
Specializing
2.112
we adopt the following definition:

Definition 2.40
.

We define the Borel-equivariant analytic
K
-homology as the composition

\hskip-8.5359ptK_{{\mathbf{C}},A}^{\mathrm{an},hG}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\xrightarrow{K_{{\mathbf{C}},A}^{\mathrm{an}}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG\times BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{diag}}_{BG}^{*}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))\xrightarrow{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

It is again an equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory.

In the following we construct the Borelification map

\beta:K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\to K^{\mathrm{an},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\ .

(2.51)

Construction 2.41
.

Recall that
\ell:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}
represents the Dwyer-Kan localization at the unitary equivalences.
We first construct a morphism

(\phi,\sigma):\ell{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\ell{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}\ .

(2.52)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1})
.
The functor
\phi:{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}
sends
the object
(C,\rho)
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
to
C
in
{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}
, and the morphism

A:(C,\rho)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
to
A:C\to C^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}
.
The cocycle
\sigma=(\sigma_{g})_{g\in G}
consists of natural transformations

\sigma_{g}:\phi\circ g\to g\circ\phi
. We define the components by

\sigma_{g,(C,\rho)}:=\rho_{g}
. Indeed,

\phi(g(C,\rho))=C
and
g(\phi(C,\rho))=gC
and

\sigma_{g,(C,\rho)}:=\rho_{g}:C\to gC
is a unitary as required.
In order to see naturality, consider
A:(C,\rho)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.
Then
\phi(g(A))=\rho^{\prime,-1}_{g}gA\rho_{g}
and
g(\phi(A))=gA
. The resulting relation

\sigma_{g,(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})}\phi(g(A))=g(\phi(A))\sigma_{g,(C,\rho)}

expresses the naturality of
\sigma_{g}
. The cocycle condition for the
\rho
’s induces the cocycle
condition for
\sigma
. This finishes the construction of the map (
2.52
).

We have a canonical functor

\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}:\mathbf{E}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{E})\ .

It is determined by the property that it sends
\mathrm{e}^{G}(A)
for
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to

\mathrm{e}(A)
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{E})
for
A
considered just as a
C^{*}
-algebra, and with the
G
-action induced by the
G
-action on
A
and functoriality of
\mathrm{e}
.
We write
\underline{\mathbf{E}}
for the morphism
KU
-module in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{E})
. Note that

\underline{\mathbf{E}}(A,B)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}\mathbf{E}(A,B)
, where on the right-hand side

G
acts by conjugation via its actions on
A
and
B
.

Note that
\mathrm{e}^{GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|G{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
sends unitary equivalences to equivalences and therefore has a natural factorization

(\mathrm{e}^{GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}_{|G{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}})_{2,1}
as in (
2.33
).
We can therefore safely omit the symbol
\ell
when we insert unital
C^{*}
-categories into
\mathbf{E}(-,-)
, or unital
G
-
C^{*}
-categories into
\mathbf{E}^{G}(-,-)
.
Furthermore it is understood implicitly that when we insert a unital
G
-
C^{*}
-category or a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra into
\underline{\mathbf{E}}
, then we apply
\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}
beforehand.
We now define the component of the Borelification map (
2.51
) at
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
as the composition

\displaystyle K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}(X)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}^{G}(C_{0}(X),A\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}}

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{E}}(C_{0}(X),A\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\eqref{fwqedwedqewdq}}

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{E}}(C_{0}(X),A\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{an},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}(X)

∎

2.6
The analytic transgression

In this section we construct the analytic transgression, a natural transformation from the equivariant topological
coarse
K
-homology of a
G
-bornological coarse space to the equivariant analytic
K
-homology of its Higson corona. We further discuss its compatibility with the Borelification maps.

We let
G_{can,max}
in
G\mathbf{BC}
be the group
G
equipped with the canonical coarse structure and the maximal bornology. Following
2.10

we can therefore consider the twist

K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A,G_{can,max}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

of the equivariant coarse topological
K
-homology from
2.29
.

We now construct the
analytic transgression as a natural transformation

T^{G,\mathrm{an}}:K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A,G_{can,max}}^{G}\to\Sigma K_{{\mathbf{C}},A}^{G,\mathrm{an}}\circ\partial_{h}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

(2.53)

We closely follow the construction from
[
BELa
, Sec. 6]
. We will use the details of the construction
of the functor
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}
from
[
BE23
]
and recalled in
2.24
.
We construct a multiplication morphism

\mu_{X}:C(\partial_{h}X)\otimes{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X\otimes G_{can,max})\to\mathbf{Q}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\ ,

(2.54)

where
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is as in
[
BELa
, Def. 2.15.(2)]
, see also
2.36
. The twist by
G_{can,max}
is introduced to make the
G
-action on

G
-bornological coarse spaces free.
This freeness is used to see that the multiplication
\mu
takes values in
\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.

Construction 2.42
.

Here are the details of the construction of
\mu_{X}
in (
2.54
). If
f
is in
\ell^{\infty}(X)
, then for any object
(C,\rho,\nu)
of
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
we can consider the element

\nu(f):=\sum_{x\in X}f(x)\nu(\{x\})

(2.55)

(strict convergence) in
{\mathtt{End}}_{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}(C)
.
The multiplication morphism
\mu_{X}
in (
2.54
) sends the object
(C,\rho,\nu)
to the object
(C,\rho)
in
\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
. Furthermore it sends
[f]\otimes A:(C,\rho,\nu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
to the morphism
[\nu(f)A]:(C,\rho)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.
If
[f]=0
, then
f
is in
\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})
and
\nu^{\prime}(f)A\in{\mathbf{C}}
by local finiteness of the objects and therefore
[\nu^{\prime}(f)A]=0
. This shows that the map is well-defined. Furthermore, if
f
is in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{B}}}(X)
, then

\nu^{\prime}(f)A-A\nu(f)\in{\mathbf{C}}
by
[
BELa
, Cor. 5.5]
. This implies that
\mu_{X}
is compatible with the composition and the involution.
∎

We further have a diagonal morphism

\delta_{\partial_{h}X}:=C(\partial_{h}X)\otimes-:\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)\otimes A)\to\mathbf{E}^{G}(C(\partial_{h}X),C(\partial_{h}X)\otimes{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)\otimes A)\ .

(2.56)

Definition 2.43
.

We define
the analytic transgression for
X
as the composition

\displaystyle T^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{X}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}(X)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)\otimes A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta_{\partial_{h}X}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{E}^{G}(C(\partial_{h}X),C(\partial_{h}X)\otimes{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)\otimes A)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{X}}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}(C(\partial_{h}X),\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\otimes A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq,\partial}}{{\to}}\Sigma\mathbf{E}(C(\partial_{h}X),{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\otimes A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\Sigma K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}(\partial_{h}X)\ .

Here
\partial
is the boundary map associated to the sequence (
2.49
). It
is an equivalence since
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is flasque
[
BELa
, Lem. 2.21]
.

Theorem 2.44
.

The family
(T^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{X})_{X\in G\mathbf{BC}}
are the components of
a natural transformation
T^{G,\mathrm{an}}
as in (
2.53
)

Proof.

The argument is non-trivial, but completely analogous to the one for the Paschke transformation given in
[
BELa
, Sec. 7]
.
∎

Remark 2.45
.

Using the composition in
\mathbf{E}^{G}
and the definitions
K_{G}(\partial_{h}X):=\mathbf{E}^{G}({\mathbb{C}},C(\partial_{h}X))
and

K_{A}^{G}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}):=\mathbf{E}^{G}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\otimes A)

we can define a pairing

-\cap^{{\mathcal{X}}}-:K_{G}(\partial_{h}X)\otimes K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A,G_{can,max}}(X)\to\Sigma K^{G}_{A}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})

which generalizes the coarse corona pairing from
[
BL24
, Def. 3.35]
to the equivariant case and coefficients (see also
Section

3.9
for an application).
∎

For
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
let
{\mathtt{pr}}:X\otimes G_{can,max}\to X
denote the projection.

Proposition 2.46
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all AV-sums.
The following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A,G_{can,max}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{pr}}\circ\eqref{erfwerfrfwreefr2334frewfr}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{pr}}\circ\beta}
\scriptstyle{T^{G,\mathrm{an}}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{pr}}\circ\eqref{vsdfvsdfvr3vsfvdfvsdfv}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{pr}}\circ\beta^{\prime}}
\textstyle{K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\circ\partial_{h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
2.51
)
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
(
2.45
)
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(T^{\mathrm{an}})^{hG}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathrm{an},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\circ\partial_{h}}

(2.57)

Proof.

We start with constructing a natural transformation

(\psi,\kappa):\underline{\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}\to\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}

(2.58)

representing the adjoint of
\beta^{\prime\prime}
in
(
2.43
).
For
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
the functor
\psi_{X}
sends
(C,\rho,\nu)
in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
to

(C,\nu)
in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
, and
A:(C,\rho,\nu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
to

A:(C,\nu)\to(C^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
. The cocycle

\kappa_{X}=(\kappa_{X,g})_{g\in G}
of natural transformations

\kappa_{X,g}:\psi_{X}\to g\circ\psi_{X}
has the components

\kappa_{X,g,(C,\rho,\nu)}:=\rho_{g}:(C,\nu)\to g(C,\nu)=(gC,g_{*}g\nu)
.
The equation
g_{*}g\nu\circ\rho_{g}=\rho_{g}\circ\nu
expresses the fact that

\kappa_{X,g,(C,\rho,\nu)}
is
{\mathtt{diag}}(X)
-controlled.
The equality
\rho_{g}\circ A=gA\circ\rho_{g}
expresses naturality of
\kappa_{X,g}
.
Finally, the family

(\psi_{X},\kappa_{X})_{X\in G\mathbf{BC}}
is the desired natural transformation (
2.58
).

The natural transformation (
2.52
) induces a natural transformation

\ell\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\ell\mathbf{Q}_{\mathrm{std}}\ .

(2.59)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1})
.
For
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
we consider the diagram

\hskip-28.45274pt\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 57.78307pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-57.78307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise G_{can,max}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 81.78307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise {\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},A,G_{can,max}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Remark 2.47
.

By specializing (

) we obtain a factorization

The dotted arrow can be considered as the best approximation of the analytic transgression by a natural transformation between

is non-trivial which implies that

is not the zero functor.
∎

2.7
Equivariant algebraic coarse
K
-homology

In this section we introduce the equivariant algebraic coarse
K
-homology and the comparison map to equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology. We discuss conditions ensuring that this comparison map is an equivalence.
We furthermore introduce the Borel equivariant algebraic coarse
K
-homology and the Borelification map using the general procedure from
Section

2.2
. We argue that the comparison map is compatible with the Borelification.

For a commutative ring
k
we let
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}
denote the category of
{\mathbf{Mod}}(k)
-enriched categories.
We let

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

denote the
algebraic homotopy
K
-theory functor for
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories described, e.g., in
[
LS16
]
,
[
Bun25
, Sec. 13.6]
.
Following
[
Bun25
, Sec. 13.8]
its twist by the non-unital algebra of trace class operators is defined by

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}:={\mathrm{Fib}}(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H({\mathcal{L}}^{1,+}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}-)\to K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H(-)):{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(2.64)

where we omitted to write the forgetful functor
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
, and the map in (
2.64
) is induced by
the canonical projection

{\mathcal{L}}^{1,+}\to{\mathbb{C}}
from the unitalization
{\mathcal{L}}^{1,+}
of

{\mathcal{L}}^{1}
.

By
[
Bun25
, Def. 13.23]
we have a natural comparison transformation

c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}:K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}\to K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ ,

(2.65)

where

K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}},\eqref{erfwrefrfwrfwrfreefrefwerferfw}}\mathbf{E}\xrightarrow{K}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

is the usual
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories. Here in the domain of (
2.65
) we omitted the functor which
interprets a unital
C^{*}
-category as a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear category, and in the target we omitted to write the restriction of the
K
-theory functor to unital
C^{*}
-categories.
By
[
Bun25
, Cor. 13.25]
the component

c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}_{\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})}
of (
2.65
) is an equivalence.
Using that any finite-dimensional
C^{*}
-algebra is Morita equivalent to some finite coproduct of copies of
\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
we conclude, using Morita invariance and additivity of the domain and target, that the
components

c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}_{\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A)}
and
c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}_{A}
for finite-dimensional
C^{*}
-algebras
A

are equivalences. Here we consider
A
as a one-object
C^{*}
-category.

For any commutative ring
k
let

\mathbf{Add}_{k}\subseteq{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}
be the full subcategory
k
-linear additive categories.
The following is taken from
[
Bun25
, Def. 6.2]

Definition 2.48
.

A functor
F:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is homological if
{\mathcal{C}}
is a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
and
F
preserves equivalences, filtered colimits, sends Karoubi filtrations to fibre sequences,
and annihilates flasques.

By
[
Bun25
, Cor. 13.22]
the restriction of
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H
to
\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
is homological. This property is inherited by the twisted version

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}
on
\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
.
Recall the functor
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
from (
2.26
).

Definition 2.49
.

We define the equivariant algebraic coarse
K
-homology functor

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\xrightarrow{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}}\mathbf{Sp}\ .

Since
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}
is homological we conclude by
[
Bun25
, Thm. 7.2]
that the functor
K^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
is indeed an equivariant coarse homology theory.

Definition 2.50
.

We define the comparison transformation

c^{G}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(2.66)

as the composition

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}=K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}\circ{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\xrightarrow{!}K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}\circ{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\xrightarrow{c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}},\eqref{vfojoivdsfvfdvswre}}K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}\circ{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}=K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}} .

(2.67)

Here the marked arrow is induced by the inclusion

{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
.

We now consider conditions ensuring that the comparison map is an equivalence.
Recall that we omit the subscript
{\mathbf{C}}
in the case that
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
with the trivial action.
Let
H
be a subgroup of
G
.

Lemma 2.51
.

The component

c^{G}_{(G/H)_{min,min}}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((G/H)_{min,min})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}((G/H)_{min,min})\ .

(2.68)

of the comparison transformation at
(G/H)_{min,min}
in
G\mathbf{BC}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

As in
[
BEKW20d
, Lem. 8.20]
observe that both,

{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((G/H)_{min,min})\ ,\qquad{\mathbf{V}}^{G}((G/H)_{min,min})

are equivalent to the categories of finite-dimensional unitary representations of
H
, considered as
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear additive category or as
C^{*}
-category.
They are equivalent to the additive completions of

\coprod_{\hat{H}^{u}_{\mathrm{fin}}}\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
in
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
or
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
, respectively,
where
\hat{H}^{u}_{\mathrm{fin}}
denotes the set of isomorphism classes of finite-dimensional unitary representations of
G
.
Since the functors
K^{{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}
and

K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}

are Morita invariant and preserve coproducts, and (
2.65
) is an equivalence on

\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
, we conclude that
(
2.68
) is an equivalence.
∎

Lemma 2.52
.

If
H
is a finite subgroup of
G
, then the component

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((G/H)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min})\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}((G/H)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min})

(2.69)

of the comparison transformation (
2.66
) at
(G/H)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min}
in
G\mathbf{BC}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

For any subgroup
H
of
G

the categories
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((G/H)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min})
and

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}((G/H)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min})
are Morita equivalent to the group ring
{\mathbb{C}}[H]

and the reduced group
C^{*}
-algebra
C^{*}(H)
, respectively,
see
[
BEKW20d
, Prop. 8.24]
,
[
BE23
, Prop. 9.12]
. If
H
is finite, then
{\mathbb{C}}[H]\to C^{*}(H)

is an isomorphism of finite-dimensional
C^{*}
-algebras.
We now use that the functors
K^{{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}
and

K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
are Morita invariant and that (
2.65
) is an equivalence on finite-dimensional
C^{*}
-algebras.
∎

Recall the universal equivariant coarse homology theory described in
2.7
.
Let

G\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}\langle\mathbf{Fin}_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min}\cup\mathbf{All}_{min,min}\rangle\subseteq G\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}

(2.70)

be the localizing subcategory
generated by the objects
\mathrm{Yo}^{G}((G/H)_{min,min})
for all subgroups
H
of
G
and
\mathrm{Yo}^{G}((G/H)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min})
forall finite subgroups
H
of
G
.
Recall from
2.7
that we denote coarse homology theories and the corresponding colimit preserving functors on motivic spectra by the same symbol.

2.51
and
2.52
imply:

Corollary 2.53
.

On the localizing subcategory (
2.70
) of
G\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}
the comparison map
c^{G}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
is an equivalence.

Example 2.54
.

If
Y
is a
G
-compact proper Hausdorff
G
-space which is a homotopy retract of a finite
G
-
CW
-complex
with finite stabilizers,
then

\mathrm{Yo}^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\max}})\otimes G_{can,min})\in\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}\langle\mathbf{Fin}_{\min,\max}\otimes G_{can,min}\rangle\ .

Here we equip
Y
with the canonical
G
-uniform and coarse structures (see
[
BELa
, Lem. 4.10]
), and
(-)_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\max}}
stands for replacing the bornology by the maximal bornology.
Note that spaces of the form
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\max}})\otimes G_{can,min}
for
G
-simplicial complexes
Y
with finite stabilizers occur in the domain of the forget-control map, see

[
BEKW20b
, Sec. 8]
.

Assume now that
Z
in
G\mathbf{UBC}

a homotopy retract of a
G
-finite
G
-simplicial complex with the
structures induced by the sperical path metric.
Then

\mathrm{Yo}^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Z))\in\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}\langle\mathbf{All}_{min,min}\rangle\ .

In both cases, using homotopy invariance, we reduce to the case of a
G
-
CW
-complex or
G
-simplicial complex. We then argue by induction over the cells using excision
and homotopy invariance. The start of the inductions are given by

2.51
or
2.52
, respectively.

∎

We define the associated non-equivariant
algebraic coarse

and the corresponding

by specializing the definitions from

2.2
to

.

(2.72)

The latter allows to define the Borelification map

\beta:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(2.73)

by
2.20

and
the Borelification of the comparison map

c^{hG}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(2.74)

It immediately follows from the construction of the transfers

This in turn gives an extension of the comparison map (

c^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}},\mathrm{tr}}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},\mathrm{tr}}:G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

From

we conclude:

.

)

Note that as in the case of equivariant coarse topological

2.8
Equivariant coarse periodic cyclic homology

In this section we introduce the equivariant coarse periodic cyclic homology theory and the corresponding Borelification. The equivariant coarse periodic cyclic homology theory will be defined by composing the periodic cyclic homology functor for
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear additive categories with the functor

{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
from (
2.26
) and forcing
u
-continuity.
In principle we could map
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear additive categories, using bounded chain complexes represening the stable hull
[
BC20
]
, to
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear
stable
\infty
-categories and then apply the general periodic cyclic homology functor defined by taking

S^{1}
-Tate-fixed points of the Hochschild homology functor for
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear stable
\infty
-categories.
But in order to construct trace maps from the
equivariant coarse periodic cyclic homology theory to periodic coarse homology later
we rely on the concrete classical chain complex model for periodic cyclic homology of
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear additive categories which we now recall.

Recall from
[
Kas87
]
that a mixed complex over a commutative ring
k
is a triple
(C_{*},d,B)
, where

(C_{*},d)
is a chain complex over
k
and
B:C_{*}\to C_{*+1}
is an additional degree-
1
map such that
dB+Bd=0
and
B^{2}=0
.
A morphism between mixed complexes is a map of chain complexes over
k
which intertwines the additional degree-
1
maps. We get a category
\mathbf{Mix}_{k}
of mixed complexes over
k
and morphisms.

A morphism of mixed complexes is called a weak equivalence if it induces a
quasi-isomorphism of underlying chain complexes. We let

\ell:\mathbf{Mix}_{k}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}

(2.79)

denote the Dwyer-Kan localization at the weak equivalences.
The
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}
turns out to be cocomplete and stable
[
Cap19
, Prop. 3.1.10]
.

We have a functor

\mathrm{Mix}_{k}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}

(2.80)

which sends a
k
-linear category
{\mathbf{A}}
to the mixed complex
associated to the cyclic nerve of
{\mathbf{A}}
considered as an object of
\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}
.

Remark 2.57
.

For
{\mathbf{A}}
in
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}

we have

\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n}\cong\bigoplus_{(A_{0},\dots,A_{n})\in\mathrm{Ob}({\mathbf{A}})^{n+1}}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(A_{0},A_{n})\otimes_{k}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(A_{1},A_{0})\otimes_{k}\dots\otimes_{k}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(A_{n},A_{n-1})\ .

(2.81)

We let
d_{i}:\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n}\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n-1}
be the
k
-linear map induced by the composition
at
A_{i}
. Thereby
d_{n}(f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n})=(-1)^{n}f_{n}f_{0}\otimes f_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n-1}
.
Then

d:=\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}d_{i}:\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n}\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n-1}

is the differential of the mixed complex in degree
n
. The map

B:\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n}\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n+1}

is given by

(-1)^{n+1}(1-t)sN
, where
t:\mathbf{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n+1}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n+1}
is the cyclic permutation sending

f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n+1}
to
(-1)^{n+1}f_{n+1}\otimes f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n}
,

N:=\sum_{i=0}^{n+1}t^{i}:\mathbf{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n+1}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n+1}
,
and the map
s:\mathbf{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n+1}
sends
f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n}
to

\mathrm{id}_{A_{n}}\otimes f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n}
.

∎

While
\mathbf{Mix}_{k}
is defined on all of
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}
its restriction to
\mathbf{Add}_{k}
is has good properties.
Recall
2.48
.

Proposition 2.58
.

If
k
is a field, then
the functor
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}
is homological.

Proof.

This assertion is surely folklore. We sketch the argument from
[
Cap19
]
.

The cyclic nerve construction, forming the associated mixed complex, and the localization
\ell
in (
2.79
)
all preserve filtered colimits. Therefore
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}
does so.

By
[
Cap19
, Lem. 3.4.4]
the functor
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}
is equivalent to the restriction
Keller’s mixed complex functor
[
Kel99
]
for exact
k
-linear categories to
k
-linear additive categories equipped with the split exact structure. In more detail,
recall that Keller’s functor associates to an exact category
{\mathbf{A}}
the object

\mathrm{Cofib}(\mathrm{Mix}_{k}(\mathbf{Acyc}^{b}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}))\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime})))

(2.82)

where
{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
is a resolution by an exact flat
dg
-category,

{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime})
is the category of bounded chain chain complexes over
{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
, and

\mathbf{Acyc}^{b}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime})
is the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime})
of acylic chain complexes.
In this formula
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}
is the obvious extension of the mixed complex functor from
k
-linear to
dg
-categories over
k
.

First of all, since we assume that
k
, is a field it is not necessary
to go over to flat resolutions. Since we consider additive categories as exact categories with the split-exact structure
the mixed complex of the
dg
-category of bounded acyclic chain complexes over the additive category is acyclic

[
Cap19
, Lem. 3.4.3]
. We therefore do not need to consider the cofibre construction above.
Finally the map
{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{A}})
sending the objects of
{\mathbf{A}}
to chain complexes concentrated in degree
0
induces a weak equivalence
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{A}}))
by
[
Cap19
, Lem. 3.4.3(i)]
.

We therefore know that the functor
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}
from (
2.80
) has the
homological properties of Keller’s functor stated in
[
Kel99
, Thm. 1.5.]
.

An equivalence of additive categories
{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
induces a weak equivalence of
infinity categories of bounded chain complexes
{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{A}})_{\infty}\to{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime})_{\infty}
(see
[
BE25
, Lem. 2.15]
)
and therefore by the exactness result
[
Kel99
, Thm. 1.5.(c)]
an equivalence

\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime})
.

By
[
Sch04
, Ex. 1.8, Prop. 2.6]
a Karoubi filtration
{\mathbf{I}}\subseteq{\mathbf{A}}
induces a Karoubi sequence

{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{I}})_{\infty}\to{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{A}})_{\infty}\to{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}({\mathbf{A}}/{\mathbf{I}})_{\infty}

which again by

[
Kel99
, Thm. 1.5.(c)]
induces a fibre sequence

\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{I}})\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}}/{\mathbf{I}})\ .

∎

We have a bifunctor

{\mathbf{Alg}}_{k}\times{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\ ,\qquad(R,{\mathbf{A}})\mapsto R\otimes_{k}{\mathbf{A}}\ .

The category
R\otimes_{k}{\mathbf{A}}
has the same objects as
{\mathbf{A}}
, and
the morphism spaces are given by

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{R\otimes_{k}{\mathbf{A}}}(A,A^{\prime}):=R\otimes_{k}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(A,A^{\prime})\ .

(2.83)

The composition involves the product of
R
. Applied to a non-unital algebra this construction would yield a non-unital category. In order to avoid this problem we adopt the following construction.

Let
R
be a possibly non-unital
k
-algebra and
R^{+}\to k
be the canonical morphism from the unitalization
R^{+}
. Then we define the
R
-twist of the mixed complex functor by

\mathrm{Mix}_{R}:={\mathrm{Fib}}(\mathrm{Mix}_{k}(R^{+}\otimes_{k}-)\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}(-)):\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}\ .

(2.84)

The following is a consequence of
2.58
.

Corollary 2.59
.

If
k
is field, then
the functor
\mathrm{Mix}_{R}:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}
is homological.

Assume that
\mathrm{tr}:R\to k
is a
k
-linear trace.

Lemma 2.60
.

The trace induces a natural transformation of functors

{\mathtt{Tr}}:\mathrm{Mix}_{R}\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}\ .

(2.85)

Proof.

The underlying
k
-module of
R^{+}
is
R\oplus k
whose elements will be denoted by
(r,\lambda)

with
r
in
R
and
\lambda
in
k
.
We can extend the trace to a trace
\mathrm{tr}^{+}:R^{+}\to k
by setting

\mathrm{tr}^{+}(r,\lambda):=\mathrm{tr}(r)
. The transformation (
2.85
) is induced by a chain-level construction
{\mathtt{Tr}}^{+}
. As explained in
2.57
and using (
2.83
) we have

\mathrm{Mix}_{R^{+}}({\mathbf{A}})_{n}\cong\bigoplus_{(A_{0},\dots,A_{n})\in\mathrm{Ob}({\mathbf{A}})^{n+1}}(R^{+}\otimes_{k}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(A_{0},A_{n}))\otimes_{k}\dots\otimes_{k}(R^{+}\otimes_{k}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(A_{n},A_{n-1}))\ .

The transformation
{\mathtt{Tr}}^{+}
sends

(r_{0}\otimes f_{0})\otimes\dots\otimes(r_{n}\otimes f_{n})

to

\mathrm{tr}^{+}(r_{0}\dots r_{n})f_{0}\otimes_{k}\dots\otimes_{k}f_{n}

in (
2.81
).
One checks that this map is compatible with the differentials
d
and
B
. The canonical homomorphism
R^{+}\to k
induces the second map in the
exact sequence of mixed complexes

0\to F\to\mathrm{Mix}_{R^{+}}({\mathbf{A}})\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})_{n}\to 0

(this time considered in
\mathbf{Mix}_{k}
), where according to (
2.84
) the kernel
F
of the second map represents
\mathrm{Mix}_{R}({\mathbf{A}})
.
The restriction of
{\mathtt{Tr}}^{+}
to
F
is a map
{\mathtt{Tr}}:\mathrm{Mix}_{R}({\mathbf{A}})\to\mathrm{Mix}_{k}({\mathbf{A}})
in
\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}

which represents the desired natural transformation.
∎

The following is a version of
[
Cap19
, Thn. 3.3.2]
and a formal consequence of
[
Bun25
, Thm. 7.2]

and
2.59
.
Let
R
be a
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebra, e.g., the algebra
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}
of trace class operators.

Theorem 2.61
.

The composition

\mathrm{Mix}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}:G\mathbf{BC}\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}}\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mix}_{R}}\mathbf{Mix}_{{\mathbb{C}},\infty}

is an equivariant coarse homology theory.

If
R={\mathbb{C}}
, then we omit this symbol from the notation.

Remark 2.62
.

One can check that
\mathrm{Mix}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}
is in addition strong and continuous. As these properties are not needed
in the present paper we will not give the argument.
∎

If
R
has a trace
\mathrm{tr}:R\to{\mathbb{C}}
, then by
2.60
we get a natural transformation of coarse homology theories

{\mathtt{Tr}}:\mathrm{Mix}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}\to\mathrm{Mix}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{{\mathbb{C}},\infty}\ .

(2.86)

We have a functor

\mathrm{PCH}^{\prime}:\mathbf{Mix}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Ch}}_{k}

(2.87)

which sends a mixed complex to the associated periodic cyclic homology complex.
The functor is finitely additive and preserves exact sequences.

Remark 2.63
.

If
(C_{*},d,B)
is a mixed complex over
k
, then the periodic cyclic homology complex
is given by

\mathrm{PCH}^{\prime}(C_{*},d,B)_{n}:=\prod_{k\in{\rm Nature}}C_{n+2k}

with the differential

\hat{d}+\hat{B}
, where
\hat{d}
is the factorwise application of
d
and

\hat{B}
is given by
B:C_{*+2k}\to C_{*+2k+1}
, where the target is considered as a factor with index
k+1

(so that
\hat{B}
has degree
-1
).
∎

The functor in (
2.87
)
preserves weak equivalences and therefore descends to a functor

\mathrm{PCH}:\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)\ .

This functor
\mathrm{PCH}
does not preserve infinite filtered colimits because of
the infinite products involved in its chain-level construction.

We define the functor

\widetilde{\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}}:G\mathbf{BC}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mix}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}}\mathbf{Mix}_{{\mathbb{C}},\infty}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{PCH}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})\ .

Since
\mathrm{PCH}
is exact this functor inherits the following properties from
\mathrm{Mix}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}
:
coarse invariance,
excision, and
vanishing on flasques.
But since
\mathrm{PCH}
does not preserve filtered colimits this composition is no longer
u
-continuous and therefore not a coarse homology theory.
But we can force
u
-continuity by
2.11
.

Definition 2.64
.

We define the equivariant coarse periodic cyclic homology theory
with coefficients in
({\mathbf{C}},R)

\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}:=(\widetilde{\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}})^{u}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})

as the functor obtained by forcing
u
-continuity on
\widetilde{\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}}
.

If
R={\mathbb{C}}
, then we omit this symbol from the notation.

Remark 2.65
.

One can show in addition that
\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}
is strong.
∎

In the presence of a trace
\mathrm{tr}:R\to{\mathbb{C}}

the transformation (
2.86
) induces a transformation of equivariant coarse homology theories

{\mathtt{Tr}}:\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}\to\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})\ .

(2.88)

Recall that

\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}:=(\mathrm{PCH}\circ\mathrm{Mix}_{R}\circ{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}})^{u}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})\ .

If we assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all AV-sums, then the extension
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},\mathrm{tr}}
from (
2.72
) provides an extension

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.17426pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.17426pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern (2.89)

We can therefore define the Borel equivariant version

\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})

(2.90)

of the equivariant coarse periodic cyclic homology and
the Borelification map

\beta:\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}\to\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})

(2.91)

by specializing the constructions from

Section

2.2
.

Note that as in the case of equivariant coarse algebraic
K
-homology
we have a non-equivariant version
\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}
and a Borelification map

\beta^{\prime}:\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}\to\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}
defined similarly as the map with the same symbol in (
2.42
)
Analoguousy to
2.32
and
2.35
one can show, as announced in
2.22
:

Proposition 2.66
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all AV-sums.
There is a natural equivalence

\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}\simeq\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\}}_{{\mathbf{C}},R}:BG\times G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})

(2.92)

under which the Borelification maps
\beta
and
\beta^{\prime}
become equivalent, i.e.,

(2.93)

commutes.

2.9
The algebraic coarse Chern character

In this section we introduce the algebraic coarse Chern character
as a natural transformation from equivariant algebraic coarse
K
-homology to equivariant coarse periodic cyclic homology. We further show that it is compatible with Borelification.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
0
. The periodic cyclic homology functor
\mathrm{PCH}_{k}
has a canonical extension to

{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
and we
have a natural transformation

\mathrm{ch}^{GJ}:K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H\to\mathrm{PCH}_{k}:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)\ .

(2.94)

Remark 2.67
.

The superscript
GJ
stands for Goodwillie-Jones and indicates that this transformation
is derived from the Goodwillie-Jones trace. The Chern character map (
2.94
) is well-known and widely used in the literature, but the detailed construction is difficult to locate in the literature.
For completeness we provide a sketch.

Note that the domain and target of (
2.94
) are defined on
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}
, and that the
Chern character can be extended to this bigger domain. But for simplicity, in the following we restrict to additive categories since we defined
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}
using the naive mixed complex functor in contrast to (
2.82
).

Let

\mathrm{CH}^{-}:\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

be the negative cyclic homology complex functor.
We have a natural transformation of functors

\mathrm{CH}^{-}\to\mathrm{PCH}:\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

(2.95)

induced by the inclusion of the negative cyclic homology complex into the periodic one.

By Morita invariance, the classical non-connective algebraic
K
-theory functor for rings over
k
is equivalent to the composition

K^{\mathbf{Ring}_{k}}:\mathbf{Ring}_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{incl}}{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\xrightarrow{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}}\mathbf{Sp}\ .

Similarly we define
the functor

\mathrm{Mix}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring}}:\mathbf{Ring}_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{incl}}{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mix}_{k}}\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}\ .

Furthermore we define the negative cyclic homology for rings by

\mathrm{CH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring},-}:\mathbf{Ring}_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mix}^{\mathbf{Ring}}_{k}}\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{CH}^{-}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

(2.96)

and
the periodic cyclic homology for rings by

\mathrm{PCH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring}}:\mathbf{Ring}_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Mix}^{\mathbf{Ring}}_{k}}\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{PCH}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)\ .

The transformation (
2.95
) induces a transformation

\mathrm{CH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring},-}\to\mathrm{PCH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring}}:\mathbf{Ring}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)\ .

Since
\mathbf{Mix}_{k}
is the naive mixed complex of the cyclic nerve of the
k
-linear category
the functors
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring}}
,
\mathrm{CH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring},-}
,
\mathrm{PCH}^{\mathbf{Ring}}_{k}
are represented by the explicit chain complex-valued constructions described, e.g., in
[
Lod98
]
.

We start from the classical Goodwillie-Jones trace

c^{GJ}:K^{\mathbf{Ring}_{k}}\to\mathrm{CH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring},-}\ .

We let

h:K^{\mathbf{Ring}_{k}}\to K^{\mathbf{Ring}_{k}}H:\mathbf{Ring}_{k}\to\mathbf{Sp}

be the universal homotopy invariant functor
under
K^{\mathbf{Ring}}
given by Weibel’s homotopy
K
-theory
[
Wei89
]
.
We then consider the square of natural transformations

\textstyle{K^{\mathbf{Ring}_{k}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{c^{GJ}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathbf{Ring}_{k}}H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ch}^{GJ,\mathbf{Ring}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{CH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring},-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring}}}

of functors from
\mathbf{Ring}_{k}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)
. Since
\mathrm{PCH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring}}
is homotopy invariant by
[
Goo85
]

[
Goo86
]

we get the dotted arrow
\mathrm{ch}^{GJ,\mathbf{Ring}}
from the universal property of
h
.

In order to get
\mathrm{ch}^{GJ}
in (
2.94
) we must now extend
\mathrm{ch}^{GJ,\mathbf{Ring}}
from rings over
k
to
k
-linear additive categories.
For more details about the following constructions see
[
Bun25
, Sec. 13.4]
.
Let
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k,i}
denote the wide subcategory of
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}
of functors which are injective on objects. We have a functor

A^{\mathrm{alg}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k,i}\to\mathbf{Ring}_{k}

which sends a
k
-linear category
{\mathbf{A}}
to the ring
A^{\mathrm{alg}}({\mathbf{A}})
whose underlying

k
-module is

\bigoplus_{A,B\in\mathrm{Ob}({\mathbf{A}})}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(A,B)\ ,

and whose composition is the obvious matrix
multiplication. The functor
A^{\mathrm{alg}}
can be restricted to
\mathbf{Add}_{k}
.
Using that
K^{\mathbf{Ring}_{k}}H
and
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring}}
are matrix stable and preserve filtered colimits one can check that
the compositions

K^{\mathbf{Ring}_{k}}H\circ A^{\mathrm{alg}}:\mathbf{Add}_{k,i}\to\mathbf{Sp}

and

\mathrm{Mix}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring}}\circ A^{\mathrm{alg}}:\mathbf{Add}_{k,i}\to\mathbf{Mix}_{k,\infty}

preserve equivalences, see
[
Bun25
, Lem. 13.20]
for an argument. Hence also
\mathrm{PCH}_{k}^{\mathbf{Ring}}\circ A^{\mathrm{alg}}:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

preserves equivalences. Note that the argument of
[
Bun25
, Lem. 13.20]

does not directly apply to periodic cyclic homology since this functor does not preserve filtered colimits.
We get factorizations

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 29.85039pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.74272pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.85039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise The transformation
\mathrm{ch}^{GJ,\mathbf{Ring}}
induces a transformation of functors

\hat{\mathrm{ch}}^{GK}:\hat{K}^{\mathbf{Add}_{k}}H\to\widehat{\mathrm{PCH}_{k}}:\mathbf{Add}_{k,i}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(2.97)

We finally use that the inclusion

\mathbf{Add}_{k,i}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k}
induces an equivalence

\mathbf{Add}_{k,i}[eq^{-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathbf{Add}_{k}[eq^{-1}]
.
