For the commutativity of the left square we refer to
[
Bun25
, Lem. 13.21]
.
The argument only depends the fact that the functors preserve filtered colimits and that

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{|\mathbf{Add}_{k}}
is Morita invariant.
The same holds true for
\mathrm{Mix}_{k}
(see
[
Kel99
, Thm. 1.5.(a)]
for Morita invariance)
which implies commutativity of the second square. This directly implies the commutativity of the
third.

The natural transformation (
2.97
) eventually
induces the desired natural transformation (
).
∎

Remark 2.68
.

For explicit calculations one often needs a formula
for the composition

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}\to K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ch}^{GJ}}\mathrm{PCH}_{k}:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

To this end one observes that
this transformation extends to a transformation of functors defined on
\mathbf{dgCat}_{k}
.
The induced transformation

\pi_{0}K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}\to\pi_{0}\mathrm{PCH}_{k}:\mathbf{dgCat}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Ab}}

is characterized by universal properties in
[
Tab11
]
.
This characterization leads to explicit formulas
as in
[
LT25
, 4.3.4]
.

∎

In view of
2.49
and
2.64
the transformation
(
2.94
) induces a natural transformation of equivariant coarse homology theories

\mathrm{ch}^{\mathrm{alg}}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(2.98)

We can post-compose with the transformation
(
2.88
) induced by the trace
\mathrm{tr}:{\mathcal{L}}^{1}\to{\mathbb{C}}
.

Definition 2.69
.

We define the algebraic Chern character transformation
as the composition

\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ch}^{\mathrm{alg}}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}},\eqref{oihbuirthbidgbdfg}}\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{Tr}},\eqref{gerwfewrfefwerf}}\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

Remark 2.70
.

We have constructed the bold part of

\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{top}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}

(2.99)

and one could ask whether there exists a factorization indicated by the dotted arrow.
Recall the localizing subcategory (
2.70
) of
G\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}

and
2.53
.

Corollary 2.71
.

We have a natural transformation

\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{top}}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{|\eqref{fqwewefedqedew}}\to\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{|\eqref{fqwewefedqedew}}\ .

At the moment we do not have an argument that
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{top}}
does not exist in general on all of
G\mathbf{BC}
. But even partial existence beyond (
2.70
) would have strong consequences.

Recall
2.53
and
2.54
.
The complex version of the Novikov conjecture asserts that the assembly map

\mathrm{asmbl}^{K^{G}}:\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}}K^{G}\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}\to K^{G}(*)\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}

is injective on homotopy groups, where

K^{G}:=K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}((-)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min}):G\mathbf{Orb}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

We define the functors

K^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}:=K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((-)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min}):G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}

and

\mathrm{PCH}^{G}:=\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}((-)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min}):G\mathbf{Orb}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})\ .

We now assume that the extension
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{top}}
exist on the image of the functor

(-)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min}:G\mathbf{Orb}\to G\mathbf{BC}\ .

(2.100)

Then we have the commutative diagram in
\mathbf{Sp}

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}}\mathrm{PCH}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.33449pt\raise-58.88506pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.93947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{asmbl}^{\mathrm{PCH}^{G}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 137.85701pt\raise-65.82452pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 70.28764pt\raise-65.82452pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 100.28764pt\raise-65.82452pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 137.85701pt\raise-65.82452pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}^{G}(*)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

By
2.53

the comparison map
c^{G}
is an equivalence. Since the targets of
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}
and
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{top}}
are

H{\mathbb{C}}
-modules the canonically induced we have
H{\mathbb{C}}
-module maps also denoted by
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}
and
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{top}}
as indicated.
For a finite group
H
the algebra
C^{*}(H)
is a finite sum of matrix algebras. Since

K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}({\mathbb{C}})\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H({\mathcal{L}}^{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{ch}^{GJ}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{PCH}_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathcal{L}}^{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{Tr}}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{PCH}({\mathbb{C}})

induces an equivalence (domain and target are both equivalent to

\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}
and the map induces some equivalence)
we conclude by additivity of the functors that also the left vertical composition is an equivalence as indicated.

By
[
LR06
, Thm. 1.7 & 1.8]
, if

E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G
has a
G
-compact
CW
-model, then
\mathrm{asmbl}^{\mathrm{PCH}^{G}}
is split injective.
We could conclude that

\mathrm{asmbl}^{K^{G}}\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}
is split injective.

Corollary 2.72
.

Under the assumption that
E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G
has a
G
-compact
CW
-model the existence of the extension
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{top}}
in (
2.99
) on the image of the functor (
2.100
) implies the complex version of the Novikov conjecture for
G
.

∎

The algebraic Chern character is induced from a natural transformation
of functors

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{ch}^{GL}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{PCH}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{Tr}}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{PCH}

(2.101)

applied to
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
. These functors are defined on
\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
and send equivalences to equivalences. If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all AV-sums, then we have the extension
(
2.40
) which in turn induces an extension

\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}_{\mathrm{tr}}:K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}},\mathrm{tr}}\to\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},\mathrm{tr}}:G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

of
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}
from
2.69
.
By
2.21
we conclude:

Corollary 2.73
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all AV-sums. The following square commutes:

\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ch}^{\mathrm{alg},hG}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}

(2.102)

The equivalence (
2.37
) restricts to an equivalence

\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{\{e\},\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:BG\times\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{2,1}

(2.103)

Applying (
2.101
), taking
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}
and restoring

-continuity we conclude:

Corollary 2.74
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all AV-sums.
We have a commutative square

\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ch}^{hG}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}^{hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\},\mathrm{ctr},hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ch}^{\{e\},hG}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\{e\},hG}}

(2.104)

The vertical equivalences in (

) are induced by (

), and the horizontal maps by (

).

2.10
Equivariant periodic coarse homology

In the section we recall the construction of the equivariant coarse ordinary homology theory, introduce its periodization and its Borelification.

We first recall the construction of the equivariant coarse ordinary homology theory from
[
BEKW20d
, Sec. 7]
.
Let
k
be a commutative ring an
A
be a
k
-module.
We consider the functor

C{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Ch}}(k)

(2.105)

which associates to
X
in

G\mathbf{BC}
the chain-complex

(C{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X,A),\partial)
of
G
-invariant locally finite controlled
A
-valued chains on
X
.
An element
\phi
in
C{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{n}(X,A)
is a function

\phi:X^{n+1}\to A
which is

1.

G
-invariant:
\phi(gx_{0},\dots,gx_{n})=\phi(x_{0},\dots,x_{n})
for all
g
in
G
and
(x_{0},\dots,x_{n})
in
X^{n+1}
.

2.

locally finite: For every bounded subset
B
of
X
and
i
in
\{0,\dots,n\}
the set

{\mathtt{pr}}_{i}({\mathtt{supp}}(\phi))\cap B
is finite.

3.

controlled: There exists an entourage
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}
such that
\phi
is
U
-controlled, i.e.,
for all
(x_{0},\dots,x_{n})
in
{\mathtt{supp}}(\phi)
and all
i,j
in
\{0,\dots,n\}
we have

(x_{i},x_{j})\in U
.

Here
{\mathtt{supp}}(\phi):=\{(x_{0},\dots,x_{n})\in X^{n+1}\mid\phi(x_{0},\dots,x_{n})\not=0\}
is the support of
\phi
,
and
{\mathtt{pr}}_{i}:X^{n+1}\to X
is the projection onto the
i
’th component.
The differential
\partial:C{\mathcal{X}}_{n}^{G}(X,A)\to C{\mathcal{X}}_{n-1}^{G}(X,A)
is given by

\partial\phi:=\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\partial_{i}\ ,

where

(\partial_{i}\phi)(x_{0},\dots,x_{n-1}):=\sum_{x\in X}\phi(x_{0},\dots,x_{i-1},x,x_{i},\dots,x_{n-1})

takes the sum over the fibre of
{\mathtt{pr}}_{i}
.
If
\phi
is
U
-controlled, then
the summand for
x
is non-zero only if
x\in\bigcap_{i=0}^{n-1}U[x_{i}]
. Since
this set if bounded and
\phi
is locally finite the sum is finite.
For
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
we have an isomorphism

C{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X,A)\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}C{\mathcal{X}}_{U}^{G}(X,A)\ ,

(2.106)

where
C{\mathcal{X}}_{U}^{G}(X,A)
is the subcomplex of
C{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X,A)
of
U
-controlled chains.

Let

\ell:{\mathbf{Ch}}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

(2.107)

be the Dwyer-Kan localization at quasi-isomorphisms.

Theorem 2.75

(
[
BEKW20d
, Thm. 7.3]
)
.

The functor

\mathrm{H}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A):=\ell C{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

(2.108)

is an equivariant coarse homology theory.

The functor
\mathrm{H}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A)
is called the equivariant coarse ordinary homology theory with coefficients in
A
. It is furthermore
continuous by
[
BEKW20d
, Lem. 7.9]
,
strong by
[
BEKW20d
, Lem. 7.10]
, and
strongly additive by
[
BEKW20d
, Lem. 7.11]
.

Recall the
2.12
of
2
-periodization.

Definition 2.76
.

We define the equivariant coarse periodic homology theory with coefficients in
A
by

\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A):=P(\mathrm{H}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A)):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)\ .

By (
2.7
) for
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
we have

\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X,A)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{X}}\prod_{k\in{\rm Nature}}\Sigma^{2k}\mathrm{H}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X_{U},A)\ .

(2.109)

Lemma 2.77
.

We have

\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)\simeq\ell\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}\prod_{k\in{\rm Nature}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}C{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{U^{n}}(X,A)[2k]\ .

Proof.

We use (
2.106
),
(
2.109
) and the fact that
(U^{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is cofinal in
{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{X_{U}}
.
We also use that
\ell
commutes with filtered colimits and products.
∎

The order of the product and the colimits in this formula is relevant.
Using
2.77
one can show:

Proposition 2.78
.

\mathrm{HP}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A)
is strong.

Proof.

As this fact is not needed in the present paper and
the proof requires to unfold definitions not recalled here we leave it as an exercise to the reeader.
∎

It has been shown in
[
BEKW20c
, Sec. 3.3]
that the equivariant coarse ordinary homology theory
has an extension

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.17426pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.17426pt\raise (2.110)

This extension induces an an extension
\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}(-,A)
of the periodic version.
We can therefore define the Borel-equivariant version

and the Borelification map

\beta:\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A)\to\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}(-,A):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

(2.111)

according to
2.20
.

We finally make the points from
2.22
explicit.

Proposition 2.79
.

We have a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}(-,A)\simeq\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{\{e\}}(-,A):BG\times\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)

(2.112)

under which the Borelification maps
\beta
and
\beta^{\prime}
become equivalent, i.e.,

\textstyle{\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime}}
(
2.117
)
(
2.111
)
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}(-,A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eqref{bsdfvsdfvsfdvsfdvsfv}^{hG}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

(2.113)

commutes.

Proof.

The map

will be construced in the proof below.
For

in

we define a natural isomorphism

which sends a chain

to the chain

0&else\end{array}\right.

in

. Applying

from (

)
this isomorphism induces the equivalence

(2.115)

where
G
acts trivially on both sides.
Periodization gives the equivalence (

).

For

in

we have canonical inclusion

\underline{C{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X,A)}\to C{\mathcal{X}}(\hat{\mathrm{Res}}^{G}(X),A)

(2.116)

of chain complexes with

-action.
Applying

and taking adjoints we get the Borelification map

\tilde{\beta}:\mathrm{H}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,A)\to\mathrm{H}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}(-,A):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(Hk)\ .

Its periodization finally induces

(2.117)

2.11
The trace transformation

In this section we introduce the trace transformation from equivariant coarse periodic cyclic homology
to equivariant periodic ordinary coarse homology theory. We further discuss its compatibility with Borelification.

Recall our standing assumption that
{\mathbf{C}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is effectively additive and admits countable
AV
-sums.
In the present subsection we assume in addition that the full subcategory
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
of unital objects admits a
G
-invariant trace
\tau_{{\mathbf{C}}}=(\tau_{C})_{C\in\mathrm{Ob}({\mathbf{C}}^{u})}
, see
[
Bun25
, Def. 9.1 & 9.8]
. The trace associates to
every
object
C
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear map

\tau_{C}:{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C)\to{\mathbb{C}}

such that
for every two morphisms
f:C\to C^{\prime}
and
f^{\prime}:C^{\prime}\to C

between objects of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}

we have

\tau_{C^{\prime}}(f\circ f^{\prime})=\tau_{C}(f^{\prime}\circ f)
(trace property), and for every
g
in
G
and
h
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C)
we have

\tau_{C}(h)=\tau_{gC}(gh)
(invariance).

Example 2.80
.

The category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})^{u}
with the trivial action has a
G
-invariant trace given by the usual matrix trace.
More generally, if
A
is a unital
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra with a
G
-invariant trace, then
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)^{u}
has an induced
G
-invariant trace.
∎

In this section we construct a transformation of equivariant coarse homology theories

\tau^{G}:\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\mathrm{HP}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,{\mathbb{C}}):G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})\ .

(2.118)

It is a modification of the trace transformation from equivariant coarse Hochschild or cyclic homology to coarse homology
constructed in
[
Cap19
]
. Variants also appeared, e.g., in
[
EL25
]
,
[
LT25
]
.

Construction 2.81
.

The trace transformation will be determined by a chain-level construction.
Recall the explicit description of the periodic cyclic homology complex of a mixed complex from

2.63
and the description of the mixed complex of an additive category from
2.57
.
The trace transformation (
2.118
) will be determined by the natural chain map

\tilde{\tau}^{G}:\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Mix}_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{U}))[2k]\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{X}}\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}C{\mathcal{X}}_{U^{n}}^{G}(X,{\mathbb{C}})[2k]\ ,

see
2.64
for the domain and

2.77
for the target, where the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded group in the domain has the differential of the periodic
cyclic homology complex.
By definition, the map
\tilde{\tau}^{G}
will preserve the factors of the products.

Let
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}
contain the diagonal.
We consider the map between the factors with index
k
. Let
(f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n+2k})
be in
\mathbf{Mix}({\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{U}))[2k]_{n}
. For
x,y
in
X
the matrix coefficient
f_{i,x,y}=\mu(\{x\})f_{i}\mu(\{y\})
is a morphism in
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
.
Hence the whole composition below is a morphism in
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
and its trace is defined.
We define

\tilde{\tau}^{G}(f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n+2k})(x_{0},\dots,x_{n+2k}):=\tau_{A_{n+2k}}(f_{0,x_{n+2k},x_{0}}\circ\dots\circ f_{n+2k,x_{n-1+2k},x_{n+2k}})\ .

Using that
\tau_{{\mathbf{C}}}
is
G
-invariant one checks that the function
\tilde{\tau}^{G}(f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n+2k})
on
X^{n+2k+1}
is
G
-invariant.

If
n_{i}
in
{\rm Nature}
is such that
f_{i}
is
U^{n_{i}}
-controlled for all
i
in
\{0,\dots,n+2k\}
, then we have

\tilde{\tau}^{G}(f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n+2k})\in C{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{U^{\sum_{i=0}^{n+2k}n_{i}}}(X,{\mathbb{C}})
. In order to see this assume that
j<k
and that

(x_{j},x_{k})\not\in U^{\sum_{i=0}^{n+2k}n_{i}}
. Then

f_{j+1,x_{j},x_{j+1}}\circ\dots\circ f_{k,x_{k-1},x_{k}}=0
since this composition propagates at most as
U^{\sum_{i=j}^{k}n_{i}}
and
U^{\sum_{i=k}^{k}n_{i}}\subseteq U^{\sum_{i=0}^{n+2k}n_{i}}
since

\sum_{i=j}^{k}n_{i}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n+2k}n_{i}
.

We check local finiteness.
If
B
is a bounded subset of
X
, then for every
i
in
I
the set of
(x_{0},\dots,x_{i-1},\hat{x}_{i},x_{i+1},\dots,x_{n+2k})
in
X^{n+2k}
such that

\tilde{\tau}^{G}(f_{0}\otimes\dots\otimes f_{n+2k})(x_{0},\dots,x_{i-1},x,x_{i+1},x_{n+2k})\not=0

and
x\in B
is finite since
only tuples
(x_{0},\dots,x_{i-1},x_{i+1},x_{n+2k})
with
x_{j}\in U^{\sum_{i=0}^{n+2k}n_{i}}[B]
for all
j\in\{0,\dots,n+2k\}\setminus\{i\}
can contribute non-trivially.

Naturality is an straightforward calculation.

One checks that
\tau\circ d_{i}=\partial_{i}\circ\tau
.
This implies that

\tau\circ d=d\circ\tau
.
Using the cyclicity of the trace one checks that
\tau\circ B=0
.
This implies that

\tau
is a chain map.
∎

Definition 2.82
.

We call
\tau^{G}
from (
2.118
) the trace transformation.

We now state the compatibility with the Borelification:

Proposition 2.83
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all AV-sums.
The following square commutes:

\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{G}}
(
2.91
)
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(-,{\mathbb{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
2.111
)
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{hG}(-,{\mathbb{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{hG}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PH}^{hG}(-,{\mathbb{C}})}

(2.119)

Proof.

One checks by unfolding definitions that
the trace map
\tau^{G}
extends to a transformation

\tau^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}:\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}},\mathrm{tr}}\to\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{\mathrm{tr}}(-,{\mathbb{C}}):G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(H{\mathbb{C}})\ ,

see (
2.110
) for the target and (
2.89
) for the domain.
To this end one must check that the chain complex level construction
is compatible with transfers along bounded coarse coverings and that
2
-morphisms of spans act as equalities.
The assertion then follows from
2.21
.
∎

Proposition 2.84
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all AV-sums.
The square

\textstyle{\mathrm{PCH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{hG}}
\scriptstyle{\eqref{gjiweorjofwewerfwerfw111}^{hG}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PH}{\mathcal{X}}^{hG}(-,{\mathbb{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eqref{jbiosdfjoivdsfvsdfvsdvdfvsdfv}^{hG}}

(2.120)

commutes.

Proof.

One checks commutativity on the chain level before applying
\ell
and
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}
.
∎

2.12
Equivariant Borel-Moore homology theories

Let

E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}

be a functor to a cocomplete stable target.
We will consider the following properties such a functor could have

Definition 2.85
.

1.

homotopy invariance: The map
E(X)\to E([0,1]\times X)
induced by the projection
[0,1]\times X\to X
is an equivalence for every
X
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
.

2.

strong excision:
E(\emptyset)\simeq 0
and
for every
X
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
and inclusion
i:Y\to X
of a closed invariant subspace with complement
j:U\to X

E(U)\xrightarrow{X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\supseteq}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}_{U}}}{{\to}}U}E(X)\xrightarrow{i}E(Y)

(2.121)

is a fibre sequence.

3.

prodescent: For every cofiltered family
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
in
G\mathbf{CH}

the canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I^{\mathrm{op}}}E(X_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}E(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\in I}X_{i})

is an equivalence.

Note that in the situation of
2.85
.
2
. we have a factorization

\textstyle{E(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\emptyset\to U}
\textstyle{E(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E(Y)}
\textstyle{E(\emptyset)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Y\supseteq\emptyset\to\emptyset}

showing that the composition (
2.121
) is canonically zero.
In
2.85
.
3
. we consider the category
G\mathbf{CH}
of compact Hausdorff
G
-spaces and equivariant continuous (everywhere defined) maps as a non-full subcategory of
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
.

Let
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be a functor to a cocomplete stable target.

Definition 2.86
.

The functor
E
is an equivariant Borel-Moore cohomology theory if it is homotopy invariant, strongly excisive and satisfies prodescent.

Let
\phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a colimit-preserving functor between cocomplete stable
\infty
-categories.

Corollary 2.87
.

If
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is an equivariant Borel-Moore cohomology theory, then so is

\phi\circ E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
.

The target of the equivariant
E
-theory functor (
2.46
)
is cocomplete and stable.

Definition 2.88
.

We define the functor

\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle C_{0}}}{{\simeq}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{comm}}\to GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}^{G}}\mathbf{E}^{G}\ .

(2.122)

Lemma 2.89
.

The functor

\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{E}^{G}

is an equivariant Borel-Moore cohomology theory.

Proof.

The functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0}

is homotopy invariant
since
C_{0}
translates homotopies in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
to homotopies in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and the functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}
is homotopy invariant.

We have
C_{0}(\emptyset)\cong 0
and hence
e^{G}C_{0}(\emptyset)\simeq 0
.
If
U
is an invariant open subset of
X
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
with closed complement
Y
, then we have an exact sequence

0\to C_{0}(U)\to C_{0}(X)\to C_{0}(Y)\to 0

in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{comm}}
,
where the first map is the extension by
0
and the second is the restriction to
Y
.
By exactness of
\mathrm{e}^{G}
we get the desired fibre sequence

\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0}(U)\to\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0}(X)\to\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0}(Y)\ .

Finally, if
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
is a cofiltered system in
G\mathbf{CH}
with
X\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\in I}X_{i}
, then we get by Gelfand duality an isomorphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I^{\mathrm{op}}}C_{0}(X_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}C_{0}(X)

in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{comm}}
.
Since
\mathrm{e}^{G}
preserves filtered colimits we conclude that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0}(X_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0}(X)\ .

By the same argument, the functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0}
actually sends all cofiltered limits in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
to colimits.
∎

Let
E:G\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be a functor. The notion of homotopy invariance and prodescent for
E
are defined as above.
In order to define the notion of an equivariant Borel-Moore cohomology for functors defined on
G\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}}
we must reformulate the excision axiom.
A pair
(X,Y)
in
G\mathbf{CH}
is an object
X
in
G\mathbf{CH}
with a closed invariant subspace
Y
. We set

E(X,Y):={\mathrm{Fib}}(E(X)\to E(Y))\ .

(2.123)

A map of pairs
f:(Z,W)\to(X,Y)
is a map
f:Z\to X
in
G\mathbf{CH}
with
f(W)\subseteq Y
.
It naturally induces a map

E(X,Y)\to E(Z,W)
.

Definition 2.90
.

E
is invariant under relative homeomorphisms if
for every map
f:(Z,W)\to(X,Y)
of pairs in
G\mathbf{CH}
that induces a homeomorphism
Z\setminus W\to X\setminus Y
the induced map

E(X,Y)\to E(Z,W)
is an equivalence.

Let
E:G\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be a functor to a cocomplete stable target.

Definition 2.91
.

The functor
E
is a compact equivariant Borel-Moore cohomology if it is homotopy invariant, invariant under relative homeomorphisms and satisfies prodescent.

We let
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})
(or

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(G\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})
) denote the full subcategories of

{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})
(or

{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})
) of equivariant (compact)
Borel-Moore cohomology theories.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category and consider the restriction

r:{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})
along the inclusion
G\mathbf{CH}\to G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
.

Lemma 2.92
.

The restriction functor
induces equivalence

r:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(G\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\ .

Proof.

We let
G\mathbf{CH}_{*/}
denote the category of pointed compact Hausdorff
G
-spaces. Then
we have an equivalence
of categories

G\mathbf{CH}_{*/}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\ .

(2.124)

The functor in (
2.124
) sends the object
(X,*_{X})
in
G\mathbf{CH}_{*/}
to the object
X\setminus*_{X}
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
, and the morphism
f:(X,*_{X})\to(Y,*_{Y})
in
G\mathbf{CH}_{*/}
to the morphism

X\setminus*_{X}\supseteq X\setminus f^{-1}(*_{Y})\xrightarrow{f_{|X\setminus f^{-1}(*_{Y})}}Y\setminus*_{Y}

in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
.

The inverse functor sends the object

X
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
to the one-point compactification
(X_{+},+)
in
G\mathbf{CH}_{*/}
, and the morphism

X\supseteq U\xrightarrow{f}Y
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
to the map
(X_{+},+_{X})\to(Y_{+},+_{Y})
in
G\mathbf{CH}_{*/}

that sends all points of
X_{+}\setminus U
to
+_{Y}
and points
u
in
U
to
f(u)
.

It is clear that the restriction sends equivariant
Borel-Moore cohomology theories to
compact equivariant Borel-Moore cohomology theories.
We have a functor

\phi:{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{CM}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{CM}^{\mathrm{op}}_{*/},{\mathcal{C}})\ ,\quad E\mapsto((X,*)\mapsto E(X,*))\ .

Using (
2.124
) for the second equivalence we form the functor

s:{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{CM}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{CM}^{\mathrm{op}}_{*/},{\mathcal{C}})\simeq{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\ .

In the following we exhibit
s
as an inverse of
r
at the level of equivariant Borel-Moore
cohomology theories.

We have a natural transformation of functors

(-)_{+}\to\mathrm{id}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\ ,\qquad X\mapsto(X^{+}\supseteq X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}_{X}}}{{\to}}X)\ .

It induces a natural transformation
of functors

E\mapsto(E\to E((-)_{+})):{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\ .

(2.125)

We have a natural commutative diagram

\textstyle{+\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+\supseteq\emptyset\to\emptyset}
\textstyle{X_{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X_{+}\supseteq X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}_{X}}}{{\to}}X}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{\emptyset\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

which after applying a functor
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
with
E(\emptyset)\simeq 0
(we say that
E
is reduced) gives a natural commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern Consequently, the transformation (
2.125
)
has a canonical factorization over a transformation

E\to(E\to s(r(E)))

for reduced functors
E
.
On functors
E
which in addition satisfy strong excision this transformation is an equivalence.

On functors
E:G\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
which are invariant under relative homeomorphisms we have an equivalence

r(s(E))\to E
given by

E(X_{+},+)\simeq{\mathrm{Fib}}(E(X)\oplus E(+)\to E(+))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}E(X)\ .

If
E:G\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
satisfies prodescent and is homotopy invariant, then also
s(E)
has these properties, too. We now assume that
E
is invariant under relative homeomorphisms and show that
s(E)
satisfies strong excision. Let

i:Y\to X
be an inclusion of a closed invariant subspace with complement
j:U\to X
.
Then we get a map of pairs

(X_{+},Y_{+})\to(U_{+},+)

which induces a homeomorphism of complements. By invariance under relative homeomorphisms we get an equivalence

E(U_{+},+)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}E(X_{+},Y_{+})\ .

Furthermore,
its target is the total fibre
of

\textstyle{E(X_{+})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E(Y_{+})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E(+)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E(+)}

and therefore also the fibre of
s(E)(X)\to s(E)(Y)
, while its domain is by definition
s(E)(U)
.
∎

We now turn attention to covariant functors.
Let
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be some functor to a complete stable
\infty
-category.
We consider the following list properties

\mathbf{P}=\{homotopy\>invariant,strongly\>excisive,prodescent,Borel-Moore\>homology\}\ .

Definition 2.93
.

A functor
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
has property
P
in
\mathbf{P}
if

E^{\mathrm{op}}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}
has the property
P
.

In constructions it is sometimes difficult to check the property of prodescent. In connection with coarse homology theories
the weaker condition of local finiteness from
2.94
is sufficient which
motivates
2.95
.

Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{X}
be the family of open invariant relatively
G
-compact subsets, i.e., open invariant subsets
W
such that there exists a compact subset
K
with
W\subseteq GK
.
If
W,W^{\prime}
are in
{\mathcal{W}}
and
W\subseteq W^{\prime}
, then we have an inclusion morphism

X\setminus W^{\prime}\to X\setminus W
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
.

Definition 2.94
.

The functor
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is called locally finite if for every
X
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
we have

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W\in{\mathcal{W}}_{X}}E(X\setminus W)\simeq 0\ .

Definition 2.95
.

A functor

E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is called a weak equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory if it is homotopy invariant, strongly excisive
and locally finite

If
(X,Y)
is a pair in
G\mathbf{CH}
and
E:G\mathbf{CH}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a functor to a stable
\infty
-category, then dually to (
2.123
) we set

E(X,Y):=\mathrm{Cofib}(E(Y)\to E(X))\ .

(2.126)

Lemma 2.96
.

An equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory is a weak equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory.

Proof.

We must show that an equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory is locally finite.
Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
.
Then

(+,+)\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W\in{\mathcal{W}}_{X}}((X\setminus W)_{+},+)

in
G\mathbf{CH}
.
Using strong excision for the first equivalence and prodescent for the second we get

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W\in{\mathcal{W}}_{X}}E(X\setminus W)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W\in{\mathcal{W}}_{X}}E((X\setminus W)_{+},+)\simeq E(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W\in{\mathcal{W}}_{X}}(X\setminus W)_{+},+)\simeq E(+_{,}+)\simeq 0\ .

∎

Example 2.97
.

We consider the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
of presentable stable
\infty
-categories.
Let
\mathcal{R}
be a symmetric monoidal presentable
\infty
-category, i.e., an object in
{\mathbf{CAlg}}(\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}})
, and assume that
{\mathcal{C}}
is in
{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}}(\mathcal{R})
. Then
{\mathcal{C}}
is enriched in
\mathcal{R}
and
we have a mapping object functor

{\mathcal{C}}(-,-):{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}\times{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathcal{R}\ .

For fixed
C
in
{\mathcal{C}}
the functor
{\mathcal{C}}(-,C)
is exact and sends colimits to limits.
If

E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}

is an equivariant Borel-Moore cohomology theory, then for any object
C
of
{\mathcal{C}}

{\mathcal{C}}(E,C):G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to\mathcal{R}

is an equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory.

∎

The category
\mathbf{E}^{G}
belongs to
{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}}({\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.
For
A
in
\mathbf{E}
and
{\mathbf{C}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, by
2.37
and
2.88
we have

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}\simeq\mathbf{E}^{G}(\mathrm{e}^{G}C_{0},{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\otimes A):G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

(2.127)

In view of
2.89
and
2.97
we conclude:

Corollary 2.98
.

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},A}
is an equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory.

In the case of the trivial group we have a classification of Borel-Moore cohomology theories and a systematic construction of Borel-Moore homology theories.
We let
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})
denote the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})
of Borel-Moore cohomology theories.
The following is a consequence of
2.92
and a result of D. Clausen (see
[
KNP
, Thm. 3.6.13]
).

Since
\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
is symmetric monoidal we can talk about dualizable presentable stable
\infty
-categories.

Theorem 2.99
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is a dualizable presentable stable
\infty
-category, then the
evaluation at
*
induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathcal{C}}\ ,\quad E\mapsto E(*)\ .

(2.128)

Proof.

The evaluation map has a factorization

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\simeq}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!,\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{CM}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle E\mapsto E(*)}}{{\simeq}}{\mathcal{C}}

(2.129)

where
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})
is the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})
compact Borel-Moore cohomology theories, and
\mathrm{BM}^{\prime}
stands for the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathbf{CH}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})

of functors which are invariant under relative homeomorphisms and satisfy prodescent (in contrast to Borel-Moore cohomology we do not require homotopy invariance).
The first map is an equivalence by
2.92
, and third equivalence
is precisely
[
KNP
, Thm. 3.6.13]
which also implies the automatic homotopy invariance
explaining the equivalence marked by
!
.
∎

Remark 2.100
.

The analogue for graded-group valued
\delta
-functors of the fact that the composition of the last two morphisms in (
2.129
)
detects eqivalences is classically well-known, see
[
Kar64
]
.
In the present paper we use the constructive power of
Theorem

2.99
providing (
2.130
).
∎

Definition 2.101
.

We call the inverse

(-)^{\mathrm{BM}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{C}})

(2.130)

of the functor
in (
2.128
) the associated Borel-Moore cohomology functor.

The functor in (
2.130
) thus associates to every object
C
of
{\mathcal{C}}
the Borel-Moore cohomology theory

C^{\mathrm{BM}}
uniquely characterized by
C^{\mathrm{BM}}(*)\simeq C
.
On compact spaces
X
the value
C^{\mathrm{BM}}(X)
is given by the evaluation of the sheafification of the constant
{\mathcal{C}}
-valued presheaf
on
\mathbf{CM}
with value
C
.

Assume that
\phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
is a colimit-preserving functor between dualizable
stable
\infty
-categories.

Corollary 2.102
.

We have an equivalence

\phi\circ(-)^{\mathrm{BM}}\simeq\phi(-)^{\mathrm{BM}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{D}})\ .

For a complete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}

we let
{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+},{\mathcal{C}})
denote the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+},{\mathcal{C}})
of Borel-Moore homology theories.

Remark 2.103
.

Let
G\mathbf{Orb}
denote the category of transitive discrete
G
-spaces, also called the orbit category of
G
.
We consider the inclusion
e:G\mathbf{Orb}\to G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
.
For finite groups and complete and cocomplete
{\mathcal{C}}

one can also show directly that

e^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\mathrm{BM}}(G\mathbf{LCH}^{+},{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{Orb},{\mathcal{C}})

is an equivalence, and also an analoguous result for cohomology theories. The reason that this, in contrast to
Theorem

2.99
, works without
stronger assumptions on
{\mathcal{C}}
is that we require homotopy invariance on the domain.
Using these results the assumption of dualizability of
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
in
2.102
could be dropped.

The details are part of an ongoing PhD project.

Let
R
be a ring spectrum and
S
denote the sphere spectrum.

Definition 2.104
.

We define the functor

(-)_{\mathrm{BM}}:{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)\to{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+},{\mathbf{Mod}}(R))\ ,\qquad M\mapsto\mathbf{Sp}(S^{\mathrm{BM}},M)\ .

(2.131)

For justification note that
\mathbf{Sp}(-,M):\mathbf{Sp}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)
is exact and sends colimits to limits.

Note that the
E
-theory category
\mathbf{E}
is dualizable by
[
BD24
]
.
In view of
2.89
,
\mathrm{e}C_{0}(*)\simeq{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}
and (
2.130
) we get from
Theorem

2.99
:

Corollary 2.105
.

We have an equivalence

\mathrm{e}C_{0}\simeq{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{BM}}:\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{E}\ .

The
K
-theory functor

K:\mathbf{E}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ ,\quad A\mapsto\mathbf{E}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}},A)

preserves limits and colimits. Using that
K({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}})\simeq KU
we get by
2.102
a homotopy theoretic description of the Borel-Moore
K
-cohomology functor:

Corollary 2.106
.

We have an equivalence

K(\mathrm{e}C_{0})\simeq KU^{\mathrm{BM}}

(2.132)

in

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.

Note that the left-adjoint in

-\wedge KU:\mathbf{Sp}\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU):forget

(2.133)

is a colimit preserving functor between compactly generated and hence dualizable stable
\infty
-categories.
We conclude from
2.102
that

KU^{\mathrm{BM}}\simeq S^{\mathrm{BM}}\wedge KU

(2.134)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU))
.

Combining
2.106
with (
2.127
) we get:

Corollary 2.107
.

We have an equivalence

K^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}\simeq\mathbf{E}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathrm{BM}},A):\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

Proposition 2.108
.

We have an equivalence

K^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}\simeq K(A)_{\mathrm{BM}}

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+},\mathbf{Sp})
.

Proof.

The UCT-class (UCT stands for universal coefficients) in
\mathbf{E}
is defined as the localizing subcategory of objects
A
in
\mathbf{E}

such that the map

\mathbf{E}(A,B)\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)(K(A),K(B))

induced by the

K
-theory functor
K=\mathbf{E}({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{E}},-):\mathbf{E}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(U)
is an equivalence in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
.
We claim that all commutative
C^{*}
-algebras belong to the UCT-class of
\mathbf{E}
.
Since every commutative
C^{*}
-algebra is the filtered colimit of its separable subalgebras and the
E
-theory functor preserves filtered colimits it suffices to see that all separable commutative
C^{*}
-algebras belong to the UCT-class.
Since a separable commutative
C^{*}
-algebra
A
is nuclear the comparison map

c:\mathbf{KK}\to\mathbf{E}
induces an equivalence

\mathbf{KK}(A,B)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathbf{E}(A,c(B))

for every object
B
in
\mathbf{KK}
.
It is now a classical fact that commutative separable
C^{*}
-algebras belong to the
UCT-class for
\mathbf{KK}
.
This gives the upper horizontal equivalence in the commutative triangle

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 25.91835pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-25.91835pt\raise it remains to see that
c:\mathbf{KK}\to\mathbf{E}
is essentially surjective.
Since the separable version
c_{\mathrm{sep}}:\mathbf{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathbf{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
of the comparison map is a Verdier quotient by
[
BD24
, Prop. 3.61]

and the map
c
above is equivalent to

{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}(\mathbf{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathrm{sep}})

it is also a localization and hence essentially surjective. This finishes the proof of the claim.

Since
C_{0}
takes values in commutative
C^{*}
-algebras the functor
\mathrm{e}C_{0}

has values in the UCT-class.
Consequently we get
an equivalence

\mathbf{E}(\mathrm{e}C_{0},A)\simeq{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)(K(\mathrm{e}C_{0}),K(A)):\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

(2.135)

We now have the chain of equivalences

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{an}}_{A}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pojgkpowergewrweferfr}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}(\mathrm{e}C_{0},A)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{rgojweorferwfrefwerf}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{btrbrerertgetg}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)(KU^{\mathrm{BM}},K(A))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gwerpokfperfwefwer}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)(S^{\mathrm{BM}}\wedge KU,K(A))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{egewfgrefwref}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Sp}(S^{\mathrm{BM}},K(A))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gwerpokjfpoweferfwerfwer}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle K(A)_{\mathrm{BM}}

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{BM}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+},\mathbf{Sp})
.
∎

We choose an equivalence

KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}\simeq\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}

(2.136)

in

.

.

K_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\mathrm{an}}\simeq\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}}

Definition 2.111
.

We define the homotopical Chern character as the composition

{\mathbf{ch}}^{h}:K^{\mathrm{an}}\xrightarrow{\eqref{gwerojopwerferfwerfwerfw}}K^{\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ggwerfrfrewfwwrefw34f}}}{{\simeq}}\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}}\ .

The projection to the factor indexed by
k
yields the component
{\mathbf{ch}}^{h}_{k}:K^{\mathrm{an}}\to\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}}
.

We now come back to the equivariant case.
Assume that
E:BG\times\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}

is such that its adjoint

BG\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathbf{LCH}^{+},{\mathcal{C}})
takes values in Borel-Moore homology theories.

Definition 2.112
.

We define the Borel-equivariant Borel-Moore homology associated to
E
by

E^{hG}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\xrightarrow{E}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG\times BG,{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{diag}}_{BG}^{*}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}}{\mathcal{C}}\ .

The justification is straightforward.

For a group
G
we can now extend the definition of the homotopical Chern character to the Borel case.

Definition 2.113
.

We define the Borel-equivariant homotopical Chern character

{\mathbf{ch}}^{hG}:K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}\xrightarrow{\beta,\eqref{gwerpoj0erjgeroigerwg}}K^{\mathrm{an},hG}_{A}\xrightarrow{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}{\mathbf{ch}}^{h}}(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}})^{hG}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

Note that
{\mathbf{ch}}^{hG}
reduces to
{\mathbf{ch}}^{h}
in the case of the trivial group.,

2.13
Equivariant Homology theories and assembly maps

We start with explaining the construction of Williams-Weiss assembly

[
WW95
]
in the equivariant context and using the
\infty
-category language.
Let
\ell:{\mathbf{Top}}\to\mathbf{Spc}
be the Dwyer-Kan localization of the category of topological spaces at the weak homotopy equivalences. This is one of the well-known presentations of the category of spaces.
We have a functor

Y^{G}:G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\ ,\quad Y^{G}(S):=\ell{\mathtt{Map}}_{G{\mathbf{Top}}}(S,X)\ ,

(2.138)

where
{\mathtt{Map}}_{G{\mathbf{Top}}}(S,X)
is the topological mapping space from the orbit
S
considered as a
discrete
G
-topological space to
X
.
A map
X\to Y
in
G{\mathbf{Top}}
is called an equivariant weak equivalence if it induces a weak equivalence
X^{H}\to Y^{H}
on the fixed points sets for all subgroups
H
of
G
.
By Elemendorf’s theorem
[
Elm83
]
the functor (
2.138
) presents its target as
the Dwyer-Kan localization of its domain at the equivariant weak equivalences
W_{weak}
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is a cocomplete
\infty
-category, then we get a functor

(-)^{\%}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{Orb},{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{\simeq,y_{!}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}({\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb}),{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{Y^{G,*}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(G{\mathbf{Top}},{\mathcal{C}})\ ,

(2.139)

where
y_{!}
is left-Kan extension along the Yoneda embedding
y:G\mathbf{Orb}\to{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})
.
Since
Y^{G}
is a localization, the functor
(-)^{\%}
is an inclusion of a full subcategory. If
{\mathcal{C}}
is stable, then the
essential image of
(-)^{\%}
is, by definition, the category of
{\mathcal{C}}
-valued equivariant homology theories.

Assume that
E:G\mathbf{Orb}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a functor to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category.
The following are well-known properties of equivariant homology theories.

Corollary 2.114
.

The functor

E^{\%}:G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is homotopy invariant, excisive for invariant open decompositions, and it sends disjoint unions of arbitrary families of
G
-spaces to sums.

Assume now that
{\mathcal{F}}
is a family of subgroups of
G
and let
G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}\subseteq G\mathbf{Orb}
denote the full subcategory of orbits with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
. Using left Kan-extension along this inclusion for the first map we define the functor

(-)^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb},{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{Orb},{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{(-)^{\%}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(G{\mathbf{Top}},{\mathcal{C}})\ .

Assume that
E:G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a functor to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category.

Corollary 2.115
.

The functor

E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}:G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is homotopy invariant, excisive for open decompositions, and it sends disjoint unions of arbitrary families of
G
-spaces to sums.

Let
i:G\mathbf{CW}\to G{\mathbf{Top}}
be the inclusion of the category of
G\mathbf{CW}
-complexes. Part of the proof of Elmendorf’s theorem
is the construction of a cofibrant approximation functor
c:G{\mathbf{Top}}\to G\mathbf{CW}
together with a transformation
i\circ c\to\mathrm{id}

implemented by equivariant weak equivalences and an equivalence

G\mathbf{CW}[W_{h}^{-1}]\xrightarrow{\simeq,i}G{\mathbf{Top}}[W_{weak}^{-1}]\simeq{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\ ,

where
W_{h}
denotes the collection of homotopy equivalences.

Let
o_{{\mathcal{F}}}:G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}\to G{\mathbf{Top}}
denote the canonical embedding.
For a functor
F:G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
we use the abbreviation.

F^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}:=(o_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{*}F)^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}:G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

As usual we will omit the symbol
{\mathcal{F}}
in the case
{\mathcal{F}}=\mathbf{All}
.

Assume now that

F
is a homotopy invariant. Its restriction to
G\mathbf{CW}
factorizes over the Dwyer-Kan localization at the homotopy equivalences and therefore gives rise to functors
F^{\prime}
and
F^{\prime\prime}
as indicated in the following diagram:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 38.39996pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.52147pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G{\mathbf{Top}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.60495pt\raise-9.78304pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.21724pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Y^{G}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 122.65472pt\raise-25.57817pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 147.06503pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.84157pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 70.60773pt\raise-42.83418pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{y}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 122.65472pt\raise-38.48355pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 150.67615pt\raise-64.04279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Then
y^{*}F^{\prime\prime}\simeq o^{*}F
.
The counit

y_{!}y^{*}F^{\prime\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}:{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\to{\mathcal{C}}
presents its domain as the final colimit preserving functor over
F^{\prime\prime}
.
Pulling this back along
Y^{G}
yields a natural assembly map transformation

\mathrm{asmbl}^{F}:F^{\%}:=Y^{G,*}y_{!}o^{*}F\simeq Y^{G,*}y_{!}y^{*}F^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{G,*}F^{\prime\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\to}}F

which presents
F^{\%}
as the final equivariant homology theory over
F
.
In order to obtain the transformation marked by
!
we argue as follows.
The cofibrant approximation transformation induces a morphism

c^{*}i^{*}F\to F
. Since
Y^{G}
sends weak equivalences to equivalences, applied to the cofibrant approximation transformation it induces
an equivalence
Y^{G}\circ i\circ c\to Y^{G}
.
We now start from the equivalence
i^{*}Y^{G,*}F^{\prime\prime}\simeq i^{*}F
which holds by definition of
F^{\prime\prime}
.
We apply
c^{*}
and get the desired map

Y^{G,*}F^{\prime\prime}\simeq c^{*}i^{*}Y^{G}F^{\prime\prime}\simeq c^{*}i^{*}F\to F\ .

More generally, for a family of subgroups we have an assembly map

\mathrm{asmbl}^{F}_{{\mathcal{F}}}:F^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\to F^{\%}\to F\ .

(2.140)

Let
F:G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be some homotopy invariant functor functor.
The following can be shown by induction over the number of cells.

Corollary 2.116
.

If
F
is excisive for open (or closed) decompositions and
X
is homotopy equivalent to a finite
G
-
CW
-complex with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
, then

\mathrm{asmbl}^{F}_{{\mathcal{F}},X}:F^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}(X)\to F(X)

is an equivalence.

We now consider the category
G\mathbf{TopBorn}
described in
Section

2.3
. We have a right Bousfield localization

i:G{\mathbf{Top}}\leftrightarrows G\mathbf{BornTop}:u\ ,

(2.141)

where
u
forgets the bornology and

i
equips a topological space with the maximal bornology.

Assume that
F:G\mathbf{TopBorn}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a homotopy invariant functor to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category.
We then define
the functor

F^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}:=i_{!}(i^{*}F)^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}:G\mathbf{TopBorn}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

If
F
is homotopy invariant, then so is
i^{*}F
and
the assembly map (
2.140
) induces a natural transformation

\mathrm{asmbl}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{F}:F^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}i_{!}(i^{*}F)^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{!}\mathrm{asmbl}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{i^{*}F}}}{{\to}}i_{!}i^{*}F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle counit}}{{\to}}F\ .

(2.142)

Lemma 2.117
.

The functor

F^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}:G\mathbf{BornTop}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is homotopy invariant and open excisive.

Proof.

In view of (
2.141
), for
E:G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
we have
i_{!}E\simeq E\circ u
.
If
E
is homotopy invariant or open excisive, then so is
i_{!}E
.
The assertion now follows from
2.115
.∎

Let
E:G\mathbf{TopBorn}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be a functor to a complete stable
\infty
-category.
Recall that a subset
W
of
X
is
G
-bounded if there exists a bounded subset
B
of
X
such that
W\subseteq GB
.

Definition 2.118
.

E
is locally finite if for every
X
in
G\mathbf{TopBorn}

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W}E(X\setminus W)\simeq 0\ ,

(2.143)

where
W
runs over the
G
-invariant
G
-bounded subsets of
X
.

Construction 2.119
.

We explain the construction of forcing local finiteness, see
[
BE20b
, Def. 7.15]
for the non-equivariant case.
Let
G\mathbf{BornTop}^{{\mathcal{W}}}
be the category of pairs
(X,W)
of
X
in
G\mathbf{TopBorn}
and a
G
-invariant
G
-bounded subset
W
.
Morphisms
(X,W)\to(X^{\prime},W^{\prime})
are morphisms
f:X\to X^{\prime}
in
G\mathbf{BornTop}
such that
f(W)\subseteq W^{\prime}
.
Let
q:G\mathbf{TopBorn}^{{\mathcal{W}}}\to G\mathbf{TopBorn}
be the forgetful functor sending
(X,W)
to
X
.
For every functor
E:G\mathbf{TopBorn}\to{\mathcal{C}}
to a complete stable target we can associate the functor

\tilde{E}:G\mathbf{TopBorn}^{{\mathcal{W}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ ,\quad\tilde{E}(X,W):=\mathrm{Cofib}(E(X\setminus W)\to E(X))\ .

(2.144)

It comes with a natural transformation
q^{*}E\to\tilde{E}
.
We define the locally finite version of
E
by

E^{\mathrm{lf}}:=q_{*}q^{*}E:G\mathbf{TopBorn}\to{\mathcal{C}}

and have a natural transformation

E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle unit}}{{\to}}q_{*}q^{*}E\to q_{*}\tilde{E}=E^{\mathrm{lf}}\ .

(2.145)

Note that

E^{\mathrm{lf}}(X)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W}\mathrm{Cofib}(E(X\setminus W)\to E(X))\ ,

(2.146)

where
W
runs over the
G
-bounded invariant subsets of
X
.

∎

Let
E:G\mathbf{TopBorn}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be a functor to a complete stable target.

Lemma 2.120
.

The functor
E^{\mathrm{lf}}
is locally finite and
E\to E^{\mathrm{lf}}
is the initial functor to a locally finite functor.

The following proposition is the straightforward generalization of
[
BE20b
, Lem. 7.35 & 7.36]
to the equivariant case.

Proposition 2.121
.

If
E
is homotopy invariant or excisive for open (closed) decompositions, then

E^{\mathrm{lf}}
is also homotopy invariant or excisive for open (closed) decompositions, respectively.

2.117
and
2.121
together imply:

Corollary 2.122
.

The functor
E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}},\mathrm{lf}}
is homotopy invariant and excisive for open decompositions.

If
E:G\mathbf{BornTop}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is homotopy invariant and locally finite, then we call

\mathrm{asmbl}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{E,\mathrm{lf}}:E^{\%,\mathrm{lf}}\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{asmbl}^{E}_{{\mathcal{F}}})^{\mathrm{lf}},\eqref{jrtzthrtzkpotrzhtzh}}E^{\mathrm{lf}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq,\eqref{ergwerfdsgwer}}}{{\leftarrow}}E

(2.147)

the locally finite assembly map for
E
and the family
{\mathcal{F}}
.

Corollary 2.123
.

If
E
is homotopy invariant, excisive for open (or closed) decompositions and locally finite, and

X
is a locally finite
G
-
CW
-complex with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
, then the locally finite assembly map

\mathrm{asmbl}^{E,\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathcal{F}},X}:E^{\%,\mathrm{lf}}(X)\to E(X)

is an equivalence.

Recall the functor
\iota^{\mathrm{top}}:G\mathbf{BornTop}\to G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
from (
2.23
).
Let
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be a functor and recall
2.95
.

Lemma 2.124
.

If
E
is an equivariant weak Borel-Moore homology theory, then

\iota^{\mathrm{top},*}E
is homotopy invariant and locally finite.

Proof.

Using the compactness of
[0,1]
one checks for
X
in
G\mathbf{BornTop}
that

\iota^{\mathrm{top}}([0,1]\otimes X)\cong[0,1]\times\iota^{\mathrm{top}}(X)\ .

Since
E
is homotopy invariant, so is
\iota^{\mathrm{top},*}E
.

In order to show that

\iota^{\mathrm{top},*}E
is locally finite we consider
X
in
G\mathbf{BornTop}
.
We must show that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W}E(X\setminus W)\simeq 0\ ,

where
W
runs over the invariant
G
-bounded subsets.
We have the inductive system
W\mapsto C_{0}(X\setminus W)
of commutative
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras
where for
W\subseteq W^{\prime}
the map
C_{0}(X\setminus W)\to C_{0}(X\setminus W^{\prime})
is given by restriction along
X\setminus W^{\prime}\subseteq X\setminus W
.
For every

f
in
C_{0}(X\setminus W)
we have

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W\subseteq W^{\prime}}\|f_{|X\setminus W^{\prime}}\|=0\ ,

where the limits runs over
W^{\prime}
.
This implies that
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W}C_{0}(X\setminus W)\cong 0
.
By Gelfand duality this is equivalent to
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W}\iota^{\mathrm{top}}(X\setminus W)\cong\emptyset
. Since
E
is locally finite in the sense of
2.94

we conclude that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{W}(\iota^{\mathrm{top},*}E)(X\setminus W)\simeq 0
.
∎

Let
E:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be an equivariant weak Borel-Moore homology theory.
By
2.124
we know that

\iota^{\mathrm{top},*}E:G\mathbf{BornTop}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is homotopy invariant and locally finite.
We get the locally finite assembly map (
2.147
)

\mathrm{asmbl}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\iota^{\mathrm{top},*}E,\mathrm{lf}}:(\iota^{\mathrm{top},*}E)^{\%,{\mathcal{F}},\mathrm{lf}}\to\iota^{\mathrm{top},*}E:G\mathbf{BornTop}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

(2.148)

2.14
Coarsification

Coarsification of locally finite homology theories has been introduced by Roe, see
[
Roe03
, Sec. 5.5]
. In this subsection we explain variants of this construction
in our contexts.
For a
G
-bornological coarse space
X
and an invariant coarse entourage
U
containing the diagonal we consider the Rips complex

P_{U}(X)
. It is defined as the realization of the simplicial complex
with
G
-action whose
n
-simplices are the
n+1
-element subsets of
X
such that each pair of elements belongs to

U
. We often interpret points in
P_{U}(X)
as finitely supported probability measures on
X
.

We realize
n
-simplices geometrically as the intersection of the standard sphere
S^{n}

with the positive quadrant of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
in order to obtain the spherical path metric structure on them. We then
equip
P_{U}(X)
with the
G
-invariant path metric which induces the spherical path metric on every simplex.
This metric
induces the uniform and coarse structure on
P_{U}(X)
. We further equip
P_{U}(X)
with the minimal compatible bornology such that the
inclusion of the zero skeleton
X\to P_{U}(X)
sending
x
to the Dirac measure
\delta_{x}
at
x
is bornologcial. In this way we will consider
P_{U}(X)
as an object of
G\mathbf{UBC}
. The inclusion of
X
as the zero-skeleton of
P_{U}(X)
is a
weak coarse equivalence
X_{U}\to\iota(P_{U}(X))
, i.e., a coarse equivalence after forgetting the
G
-action, where
\iota:G\mathbf{UBC}\to G\mathbf{BC}
is the forgetful functor.

Assume that
f:X\to X^{\prime}
is a morphism in
G\mathbf{BC}
and
U^{\prime}
is an invariant coarse entourage of
X^{\prime}
containing the diagonal such that
f(U)\subseteq U^{\prime}
. Then we get an induced map

P(f):P_{U}(X)\to P_{U^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
induced by the push-forward of probablity measures. It is a morphism of
G
-simplicial complexes and hence contractive with respect to the spherical path metrics. Since
f
is proper,
P(f)
is a morphism in

G\mathbf{UBC}
.
Assume that

F:G\mathbf{UBC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a functor to a complete stable target.

Definition 2.125
.

We define the coarsification of
F
to be the functor

F\mathbf{P}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ ,\quad X\mapsto\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{X}}F(P_{U}(X))\ .

Remark 2.126
.

In order to make this definition more precise we proceed similarly as in
2.11
.
We have already defined the functors indicated by bold arrows in

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 34.48581pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.27843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G\mathbf{BC}^{{\mathcal{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.48581pt\raise-16.06834pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(X,U)\mapsto X}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.77557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.60405pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(X,U)\mapsto P_{U}(X)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.27843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.27843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.27843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
