(3.27)

It describes two natural transformatoins

\phi,\psi:K{\mathcal{X}}_{G_{can,max}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\to\Sigma(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}})^{hG}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(-)):G\mathbf{UBC}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

where
\phi
is the clockwise, and
\psi
is the counterclockwise path. Note that
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{G_{can,max}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is an equivariant strong coarse homology theory, and that

\Sigma(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}})^{hG}:G\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to\mathbf{Sp}

is an equivariant Borel-Moore homology theory. By
3.31
the
domain and target are normal equivariant local homology theories and can be interpreted as colimit preserving functors
from
G\mathbf{Sp}\mathcal{N}
to
\mathbf{Sp}
.

We now consider the case of a trivial group
G
. Then the diagram (
3.27
) specializes to (
1.13
).
In
3.3
we have chosen the normalization of
{\mathbf{ch}}^{h}
such that
\phi_{*}\simeq\psi_{*}
.
We apply the general theory from
Section

3.7
to
{\mathcal{G}}=\{*\}
. There is a unique colimit-preserving functor

\hat{*}:\mathbf{Spc}\to\mathbf{Sp}\mathcal{N}

(3.28)

sending
*
to
\mathrm{Yo}\mathcal{N}(*)
.

Corollary 3.32
.

We have
\phi_{D}\simeq\psi_{D}
for every
D
in the essential image of
\hat{*}
.

Note that a finite CW-complex is a compact Hausdorff space and therefore canonically an object of
\mathbf{UBC}
.
We have an inclusion
\mathbf{CW}^{\mathrm{fin}}\to\mathbf{UBC}
.

Lemma 3.33
.

We have a lift

\textstyle{\mathbf{Spc}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{*}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{CW}^{\mathrm{fin}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{UBC}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Yo}\mathcal{N}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Sp}\mathcal{N}}

Proof.

We have
a factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern where the dotted arrow exists by the universal property of the marked arrow being the Dwyer-Kan localization at the homotopy equivalences since the composition

\mathbf{CW}^{\mathrm{fin}}\to\mathbf{UBC}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{Yo}\mathcal{N}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{Sp}\mathcal{N}

is homotopy invariant. Since the latter is also
excisive for cell-attachements the dotted arrow preserves finite colimits.
This implies that the lower triangle also commutes.
The assertion of the lemma follows.
∎

By combining
3.27
and
3.33
we can conclude:

Corollary 3.34
.

The restriction of the diagram (
1.13
) to
\mathbf{CW}^{\mathrm{fin}}
commutes, i.e., we
have
\phi_{|\mathbf{CW}^{\mathrm{fin}}}\simeq\psi_{|\mathbf{CW}^{\mathrm{fin}}}
.

3.9
Pairing with cohomology classes

In this subsection we evaluate the right corners of (
1.2
) on appropriate cohomology classes and discuss the consequences of the commutativity of this square.

Let
R
be a commutative ring spectrum and
M
be in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)
. Then according to
2.101
we can form the Borel-Moore cohomology theory

R^{\mathrm{BM}}:L\mathbf{LCH}^{+,\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)\ .

The dual Borel-Moore homology theory
2.104
can be written, using
R^{\mathrm{BM}}\simeq S^{\mathrm{BM}}\wedge R
, as

M_{\mathrm{BM}}:=\mathbf{Sp}(S^{\mathrm{BM}},M)\simeq{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)(R^{\mathrm{BM}},M):\mathbf{LCH}^{+}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)\ .

Hence for
Y
in
\mathbf{LCH}^{+}
we get a binatural evaluation pairing

{\mathtt{ev}}:M_{\mathrm{BM}}(Y)\otimes_{R}R^{\mathrm{BM}}(Y)\to M\ .

(3.29)

We now consider
K
-theory with values in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
. By
2.106
and
2.108
we have the equivalences

K(C_{0}(-))\simeq KU^{\mathrm{BM}}\ ,\quad\mbox{and}\quad K^{\mathrm{an}}\simeq KU_{\mathrm{BM}}\ ,

where
K:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
is the topological
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras, written as
K\circ e
in
2.106
. Hence by specializing (
3.29
) we have a bi-natural pairing

K^{\mathrm{an}}(Y)\otimes_{KU}K(C_{0}(Y))\to KU\ .

(3.30)

For
X
in
\mathbf{BC}
we have the coarse corona pairing

-\cap^{{\mathcal{X}}}-:K{\mathcal{X}}(X)\otimes_{KU}K(C_{0}(\partial_{h}X))\to K{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{B}})

from
[
BL24
, Def. 3.35]
.
The restrictions of the canonical map
X\to*
of sets to bounded subsets of
X
are a morphisms in
\mathbf{BC}
and hence induce a morphism
K{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{B}})\to K{\mathcal{X}}(*)\simeq KU
.

Lemma 3.35
.

The following square commutes:

\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}(X)\otimes_{KU}K(C_{0}(\partial_{h}X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{-\cap^{{\mathcal{X}}}-}
\textstyle{\Sigma K^{\mathrm{an}}(\partial_{h}X)\otimes K(C_{0}(\partial_{h}X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
3.30
)
\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{B}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma KU}

Proof.

By
[
BL24
, Def. 3.35]
and using the pairing
\nu

from
[
BL24
, Def. 3.34]

the down-right composition is given by

\displaystyle K{\mathcal{X}}(X)\otimes_{KU}K(C_{0}(\partial_{h}X))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{V}}(X))\otimes\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},C(\partial_{h}X))

(3.31)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{V}}(X))\otimes C(\partial_{h}X))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{V}}(X)/{\mathbf{V}}({\mathcal{B}}\subseteq X))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\Sigma\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{V}}({\mathcal{B}}\subseteq X))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Sigma KU\ .

The right-down composition is, according to
2.43
, given by

\displaystyle K{\mathcal{X}}(X)\otimes_{KU}K(C_{0}(\partial_{h}X))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{V}}(X))\otimes\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},C(\partial_{h}X))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta_{X}}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}(C(\partial_{h}X),{\mathbf{V}}(X)\otimes C(\partial_{h}X))\otimes\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},C(\partial_{h}X))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}(C(\partial_{h}X),\mathbf{Q})\otimes\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},C(\partial_{h}X))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\Sigma\mathbf{E}(C(\partial_{h}X),{\mathbb{C}})\otimes\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},C(\partial_{h}X))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{feroifjowerfwerfew}}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\Sigma\mathbf{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Sigma KU

Here
\mathbf{Q}
is the Calkin category
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})/\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
.
These maps are equivalent. In order to see this
we observe that if we compose
\nu
with
{\mathbf{V}}(X)/{\mathbf{V}}({\mathcal{B}}\subseteq X)\to\mathbf{Q}
, then we get the pairing
\mu
.
Furthermore, the composition of the pairing (
3.30
) with the diagonal morphism
can be reduced to the application of the symmetric monoidal structure as in step marked by
(1)
in (
3.31
).
∎

By (
2.136
) the target of the homotopical Chern character (
2.111
) can be rewritten as follows:

\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}}\simeq(KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathrm{BM}}\ .

We therefore have a bi-natural pairing

\hskip-14.22636pt(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{BM}})(Y)\otimes(KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{BM}}(Y)\simeq(KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathrm{BM}}(Y)\otimes_{KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}}(KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{BM}}(Y)\to KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}\ .

(3.32)

The morphism of ring spectra
\epsilon:KU\to KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}
induced by the unit of
H{\mathbb{C}}

induces a cohomological topological Chern character

\mathrm{ch}_{h}:K(C_{0}(-))\simeq KU^{\mathrm{BM}}\to(KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{BM}}

The following is a formal consequence of the definitions:

Lemma 3.36
.

The following square commutes

Corollary 3.38
.

If
x
is in
\pi_{i}K{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
and
\xi
is in
\pi_{-j}K(C_{0}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y)))
, then
we have

\epsilon(c(x)\cap^{{\mathcal{X}}}\pi^{*}\xi)=\langle T^{\mathrm{top}}P(\chi_{\mathrm{BM}})\tau\mathrm{ch}^{\mathrm{alg}},\mathrm{ch}_{h}(\xi)\rangle

(3.33)

in
\pi_{i-j}(KU)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}
.

Remark 3.39
.

The equality (
3.33
)
is in particular an integrality statement for its right-hand side.
The equality (
3.33
) is our version of
[
EL25
, (1.1)]
.
In
[
EL25
]
the cycle
x
is given in terms of operators on an
X
-controlled
Hilbert space and
\mathrm{ch}_{h}(\xi)
is described in terms of a Čech-type decomposition of
Y
.
In this case the right-hand side of (
3.33
) is made explicit
and expressed in terms of an integral over
X
of a term involving the integral kernels of the operators involved in
x
and functions describing the decomposition of
Y
.
It was one of the main goals of
[
EL25
]
to verify
the integrality of this integral (which in their case is a priori given) by interpreting it as the right-hand side of (
3.33
)
and comparison with the left-hand side.
∎

3.10
Two Chern characters

The construction of Chern character maps out of analytic
K
-homology
has a long history and motivated the introduction of cyclic homology
[
Con85
]
.
Using the universal property of the equivariant
KK
-theory functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{KK}^{G}
, constructions of Chern characters to versions of bivariant cyclic
homology have been considered in
[
Cun97
]
,
[
Nis93
]
,
[
Pus03
]
,
[
Voi07
]
.
In the present paper we added a version
\mathrm{ch}^{h}
described in
2.111
which does not involve cyclic homology at all. In this section we compare it with a more algebraic version involving cyclic homology.

The inner square in (
1.16
) is a natural (we do not say natural commutative) square

\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{G_{can,max}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{G,\mathrm{an}}c^{G}}
\scriptstyle{P(\chi_{BM})^{hG}{\mathtt{pr}}\beta\tau^{G}\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}}
\textstyle{\Sigma K^{G,\mathrm{an}}\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{ch}}^{h,hG}\beta}
\textstyle{(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbf{C}}_{BM}\mathbf{P})^{hG}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{G,\mathrm{top}}}
\textstyle{\Sigma(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{BM})^{hG}\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}}

(3.34)

of
\mathbf{Sp}
-valued functors defined on
G\mathbf{UBC}
.
Since the functors on the right-hand side are locally finite we can factorize the horizontal maps as follows:

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces K{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{G_{can,max}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P(\chi_{BM})^{hG}{\mathtt{pr}}\beta\tau^{G}\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces(K{\mathcal{X}}_{G_{can,max}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty})^{\mathrm{lf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b:=(P(\chi_{BM})^{hG}{\mathtt{pr}}\beta\tau^{G}\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}})^{\mathrm{lf}}}
\scriptstyle{a:=p^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}}c^{G,\mathrm{lf}}}
\textstyle{\Sigma K^{G,\mathrm{an}}\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ch}^{h,hG}\beta}
\textstyle{(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbf{C}}_{BM}\mathbf{P})^{hG}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{((\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbf{C}}_{BM}\mathbf{P})^{hG}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty})^{\mathrm{lf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\Sigma(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{BM})^{hG}\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}}

(3.35)

If
Y
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
is such that the component
a_{Y}
is an equivalence, then the composition

\mathrm{ch}^{G,\prime}_{Y}:=c_{Y}b_{Y}a_{Y}^{-1}:\Sigma K^{G,\mathrm{an}}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y))\to\Sigma(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{BM})^{hG}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y))

is an alternative to the Borel-equivariant Chern character
\mathrm{ch}^{h,hG}\beta
.

By
[
BELa
, Thm. 1.5]
we know that

p^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{Y}
and
c^{G}
are equivalences if

Y
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
is homotopy equivalent to a
G
-finite
G
-simplicial complex with finite stabilizers.
This implies that
p^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}}_{Y}
and
c^{G,\mathrm{lf}}
are equivalences if
Y
is homotopy equivalent to a locally finite

G
-simplicial complex with proper
G
-action.

Corollary 3.40
.

\mathrm{ch}^{G,\prime}_{Y}:\Sigma K^{G,\mathrm{an}}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y))\to\Sigma(\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}_{BM})^{hG}(\iota^{\mathrm{top}}_{u}(Y))

is defined provided
Y
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
is homotopy equivalent to a locally finite

G
-simplicial complex with proper
G
-action.

Recall that
\mathrm{ch}^{h,hG}\beta
is defined by homotopy theory, using the identification of analytic
K
-homology with the Borel-Moore homology generated by
KU
and the identification
KU\wedge H{\mathbb{C}}\simeq\prod_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Sigma^{2k}H{\mathbb{C}}
. In contrast, at least philosopically,
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\prime}
applies the algebraic Chern character
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\mathrm{alg}}
to a
suitable algebraic cycle like a summable Fredholm module. There is no obvious reason why these
constructions should give the same results.

Problem 3.41
.

Do we have an equivalence
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\prime}_{Y}\simeq\mathrm{ch}^{h,hG}_{Y}\beta_{Y}
for
Y
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
which is homotopy equivalent to locally finite
G
-simplicial complex with finite stabilizers.

The case of a finite group follows from
1.5
applied to
X={\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
and
\pi
the component of (
1.12
) at
Y
.

Corollary 3.42
.

If
G
is finite, then
\mathrm{ch}^{G,\prime}_{Y}\simeq\mathrm{ch}^{h,hG}_{Y}\beta_{Y}
for every
Y
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
which is homotopy equivalent to a locally finite
G
-simplicial complex.
